Proposition 3.11

(
[
HL15
,
FO21
,
KO23a
]
)
.

The quiver
\Gamma^{Q}
realizes the convex partial order
\prec_{[Q]}
in the following sense
:

{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\prec_{[Q]}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}
if and only if there exists a path in
\Gamma^{Q}
starting from
\phi_{Q}^{-1}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)
to

\phi_{Q}^{-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
.

We say that a total ordering
\bm{(}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{1},\ldots{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{\ell}\bm{)}
of
\Phi_{+}
is a
compatible reading
of
\Gamma^{Q}
if we have
k<l
whenever there is a path from
\beta_{l}
to
\beta_{k}

in
\Gamma^{Q}
.

Proposition 3.12

(
[
OS19
]
)
.

\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell})
is a reduced sequence of
w_{\circ}
in
[Q]
if and only if there exists a compatible reading
\Phi_{+}=\bm{(}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{1},\ldots{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{\ell}\bm{)}
of
\Gamma^{Q}

such that
i_{k}=\mathrm{res}^{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k})
for all
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
.

Proposition 3.13

(
[
HL15
,
FO21
,
KO23a
]
)
.

Let
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
be a Dynkin quiver. Then we have

(a)

\Gamma^{Q}_{0}=\{(i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}\ |\ \xi_{i^{*}}-\mathsf{h}<p\leqslant\xi_{i}\}
,

(b)

\phi_{Q}(i^{*},p\pm\mathsf{h})=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},k\mp 1)
if
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},k)
,

(c)

for
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}
with
\phi_{Q}^{-1}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)=(i,p)
, we have
\mathrm{res}^{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=i
.

By Theorem
3.5
and Proposition
3.13
, we have an automorphism
\mathfrak{D}_{q}
on
\mathcal{X}_{q}
given by

(3.6)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{q}(\widetilde{X}_{i,p})=\widetilde{X}_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}.

Since
\mathfrak{D}_{q}(\widetilde{B}_{i,p})=\widetilde{B}_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}
, it induces an automorphism on
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
also.

We denote by
\mathfrak{D}
the automorphism on
\mathcal{X}
satisfying

\mathfrak{D}\circ{\operatorname{ev}}_{q=1}={\operatorname{ev}}_{q=1}\circ\mathfrak{D}_{q}
. Then we have
\mathfrak{D}_{q}(F_{q}(m))=F_{q}(\mathfrak{D}(m))
for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}
.

Definition 3.14
.

(cf.
[
FM01
, Proposition 6.18]
)
.

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, we call

\mathfrak{D}^{k}_{q}(F_{q}(m))
the
k
-dual
of
F_{q}(m)
.

Recall that

L_{q}(X_{i,p})=E_{q}(X_{i,p})=F_{q}(X_{i,p})\qquad\text{ for all }(i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0},

by definition.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
(i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
. Then we have

(3.7)

\displaystyle F_{q}(X_{i,p})*F_{q}(X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}})=q_{i}^{-2}F_{q}(X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}})*F_{q}(X_{i,p})+(1-q_{i}^{-2}).

Proof.

Take the Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)

such that
i
is a source of
Q
with
\xi_{i}=p
. Then we have
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},0)
,
\phi_{Q}(i^{*},p+\mathsf{h})=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},-1)
and hence
{\rm wt}_{Q}(F_{q}(X_{i,p}))={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}
,
{\rm wt}_{Q}(F_{q}(X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}))=-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}
.

Rewriting the RHS of (
3.7
) as

\displaystyle(F_{q}(X_{i,p})-\underline{X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}^{-1}}+\underline{X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}^{-1}})*(F_{q}(X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}})-\underline{X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}}+\underline{X_{i^{*},p+\mathsf{h}}}),

our assertion follows from Theorem
2.7
(
d
) and Proposition
3.8
.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
(i,p),(j,s)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
with
p-s>\mathsf{h}
. Then we have

(3.8)

\displaystyle F_{q}(X_{i,p})*F_{q}(X_{j,s})=q^{-({\rm wt}_{Q}(X_{i,p}),{\rm wt}_{Q}(X_{j,s}))}F_{q}(X_{j,s})*F_{q}(X_{i,p}),

for any Dynkin quiver
Q
.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.7
(
d
) and Proposition
3.8
,
F_{q}(X_{i,p})
and
F_{q}(X_{j,s})

q
-commute as asserted.
∎

3.4.
Heart subrings

Proposition 3.17

(
[
FHOO22
,
JLO23
]
)
.

Let
\mathsf{S}
be a convex subset of
{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
, and

\mathcal{M}^{\mathsf{S}}
be the set of all monomials
m
satisfying the following condition:

(3.9)

X_{i,p}^{\pm 1}
is a factor of
m\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathsf{S}}
if and only if
(i,p)\in\mathsf{S}
.

Then we have

(a)

\mathfrak{K}_{q,\mathsf{S}}\hskip-2.15277pt\mathbin{:=}\hskip-6.02773pt\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-4.30554pt\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]F_{q}(m)=\hskip-6.02773pt\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-4.30554pt\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]E_{q}(m)=\hskip-6.02773pt\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-4.30554pt\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]L_{q}(m)
, where
\mathcal{M}^{\mathsf{S}}_{+}\mathbin{:=}\mathcal{M}^{\mathsf{S}}\cap\mathcal{M}_{+}
,

(b)

\mathfrak{K}_{q,\mathsf{S}}
is a subring of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
.

For a Dynkin quiver
Q
of
\mathfrak{g}
, let

\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}\mathbin{:=}\mathfrak{K}_{q,\Gamma_{0}^{Q}}.

By the above proposition, as a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra,
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}

is generated by
F_{q}(X_{i,p})
’s such that
(i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}
. It is known that, for any
(j,s)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
, there exists
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
and
(i,p)\in\Gamma_{0}^{Q}
such that

(3.10)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{k}(X_{i,p})=X_{j,s}.

Since
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
is generated by

\{F_{q}(X_{i,p})\ |\ (i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}\}=\{F_{q}(\mathfrak{D}^{k}(X_{i,p}))\ |\ (i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0},\;k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}\},

we call
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}
the
heart subring of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
associated to
Q
.

Using the unitriangular maps between bases of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
and the characterization of
\mathsf{L}_{q}
, we obtain the following lemma by applying the arguments in
[
FHOO22
, Lemma 3.11]
:

Lemma 3.18
.

For
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}
, we have

\mathfrak{D}_{q}(L_{q}(m))=L_{q}(\mathfrak{D}(m)).

3.5.
Quantum folded
T
-system among KR-polynomials

For
(i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\mathbin{:=}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 1}
, we define a dominant monomial in
\mathcal{X}
as follows:

m_{k,p}^{(i)}\mathbin{:=}m^{(i)}[p,s]\mathbin{:=}X_{i,p}X_{i,p+2}\cdots X_{i,p+2k-2},\qquad\text{ where }s\mathbin{:=}p+2k-2.

We call such monomials
KR-monomials
. For a KR-monomial
m
, we call
F_{q}(m)
the
KR-polynomial
associated with
m
.

Note that there exists a functional relation among KR-polynomials, called
quantum folded
T
-system
, as follows:

Theorem 3.19

(
[
HL15
,
JLO23
]
)
.

For
(i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
with
k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
, we have

(3.11)

\displaystyle F_{q}(m_{k,p}^{(i)})*F_{q}(m_{k,p+2}^{(i)})=q^{\kappa}F_{q}(m_{k+1,p}^{(i)})*F_{q}(m_{k-1,p}^{(i)})+q^{\zeta}\prod_{j\sim i}F_{q}(m_{k,p+1}^{(j)})^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}},

for some
\kappa,\zeta\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.

We remark that the half-integers
\kappa
and
\zeta
could be determined explicitly (e.g. see
[
JLO23
, Theorem 6.8]
).
The conjecture below for simply-laced type
\mathfrak{g}
is proved by Nakajima
[
Nak04
]
:

Conjecture 1

(
[
JLO23
, Conjecture 2]
)
.

For every KR-polynomial
F_{q}(m)
, we have

F_{q}(m)=L_{q}(m).

3.6.
Truncation

Let
\xi
be a height function on
\triangle
and recall the convex subset
{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
of
{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
. By Proposition
3.17
, we have the subring

\mathfrak{K}_{q,\leqslant\xi}\mathbin{:=}\mathfrak{K}_{q,{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.60275pt{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}}
. We denote by
\mathcal{X}_{q,\leqslant\xi}
the quantum subtorus of
\mathcal{X}_{q}
generated by
\widetilde{X}_{i,p}
for
(i,p)\in{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
.

For an element
x\in\mathcal{X}_{q}
, we denote by
x_{\leqslant\xi}
the element of
\mathcal{X}_{q,\leqslant\xi}
obtained from
x
by discarding all the monomials containing the factors
\widetilde{X}_{i,p}^{\pm 1}
with
(i,p)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}\setminus{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
.
We define a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-linear map
(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}
by

\displaystyle(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.95818pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.95818pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.48589pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.48589pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{q,\leqslant\xi},}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-5.85764pt\raise-8.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.85764pt\raise-8.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.46838pt\raise-8.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 34.46838pt\raise-8.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{x_{\leqslant\xi}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Proposition 3.20
.

(
[
FHOO22
, Proposition 5.11]
,
[
JLO23
, Proposition 6.3]
)
For a height function
\xi
on
\triangle
, the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-linear map
(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}
induces
the injective
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra homomorphism

(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}:\mathfrak{K}_{q,\leqslant\xi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{q,\leqslant\xi}.

Definition 3.21
.

For each Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
, we denote by
\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}
the quantum subtorus of
\mathcal{X}_{q}
generated by
\widetilde{X}_{i,p}
for
(i,p)\in\Gamma_{0}^{Q}
.

Corollary 3.22
.

Let
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
be a Dynkin quiver.
The
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-linear map
(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}
induces the injective
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra homomorphism

\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}
is generated by
F_{q}(X_{i,p})
for
(i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}
, it is enough to show that

(F_{q}(X_{i,p}))_{\leqslant\xi}
is contained in
\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}
. Then the assertion follows from Theorem
2.7
(
d
).
∎

Remark 3.23
.

Through the injective homomorphism
(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}
, the basis
\mathsf{L}_{q,Q}=\{L_{q}(m)\ |\ m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}\}
of
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}

transforms to the basis

(3.12)

\mathsf{L}_{q,Q}^{T}\mathbin{:=}\{L_{q}(m)_{\leqslant\xi}\mathbin{:=}(L_{q}(m))_{\leqslant\xi}|\ m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}\}
of
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}\mathbin{:=}(\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q})_{\leqslant\xi}\subset\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}
,

which can be characterized as follows:

(i)

\overline{L_{q}(m)_{\leqslant\xi}}=L_{q}(m)_{\leqslant\xi}
and

(ii)

L_{q}(m)_{\leqslant\xi}-E_{q}(m)_{\leqslant\xi}\in\displaystyle\sum_{m^{\prime}\prec_{\mspace{-2mu}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{\scalebox{.6}{${\rm N}$}}}m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}}q\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q]E_{q}(m^{\prime})_{\leqslant\xi}
,
where
E_{q}(m^{\prime})_{\leqslant\xi}\mathbin{:=}(E_{q}(m^{\prime}))_{\leqslant\xi}
.

4.
Unipotent quantum coordinate algebra

In this section, we review the theory of the unipotent quantum coordinate algebra, especially their elements called unipotent quantum minors, its quantum cluster algebra structure,
the dual-canonical/upper-global basis and dual PBW bases.

4.1.
Unipotent quantum coordinate algebra

In this subsection, we briefly recall the quantum unipotent coordinate algebra. We mainly refer to
[
KKKO18
,
HO19
]
and sometimes skip details available in the references.

For
n\geqslant m\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 1}
and
i\in I
, we set

q_{i}\mathbin{:=}q^{d_{i}},\ \ [n]_{i}\mathbin{:=}\dfrac{q_{i}^{n}-q_{i}^{-n}}{q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1}},\ \ [n]_{i}!=\prod_{k=1}^{n}[k]_{i}\ \text{ and }\ \left[\begin{matrix}n\\
m\end{matrix}\right]_{i}\mathbin{:=}\dfrac{[n]_{i}!}{[m]_{i}![n-m]_{i}!}.

We denote by
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
the quantum group associated to a finite
Cartan datum
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{P},\Pi,\mathsf{P}^{\vee},\Pi^{\vee})
, which is the
associative algebra over
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
generated by
e_{i},f_{i}

(i\in I)
and
q^{h}
(
h\in\mathsf{P}^{\vee}
). We set
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
(resp.
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})
) be
the subalgebra generated by
e_{i}
(resp.
f_{i}
) for
i\in I
. Note
that
e_{i}
’s (resp.
f_{i}
’s) satisfy the quantum Serre relations

(4.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{k}\left[\begin{matrix}1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\\
k\end{matrix}\right]_{i}f_{i}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-k}f_{j}f_{i}^{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{k}\left[\begin{matrix}1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\\
k\end{matrix}\right]_{i}e_{i}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-k}e_{j}e_{i}^{k}=0,

and

\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
admits the weight space decomposition:

\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})=\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\mathsf{Q}}\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}.

For any
i\in I
, there exist
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
-linear endomorphisms
{}_{i}e^{\prime}
and
e_{i}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})
uniquely determined by

(4.2)

\displaystyle{}_{i}e^{\prime}(f_{j})=\delta_{i,j},

\displaystyle{}_{i}e^{\prime}(xy)={}_{i}e^{\prime}(x)\;y+q^{({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})}x\;{}_{i}e^{\prime}(y),

\displaystyle e_{i}^{\prime}(f_{j})=\delta_{i,j},

\displaystyle e_{i}^{\prime}(xy)=q^{({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},\eta)}e_{i}^{\prime}(x)\;y+x\;e^{\prime}_{i}(y),

where
x\in\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mspace{1mu}\beta},\ y\in\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})_{\eta}
.
Then it is known
[
Kas91
]
that there exists a unique non-degenerate symmetric
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
-bilinear form
(,)_{K}
on
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})
such that

(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1})_{K}=1,\quad(f_{i}u,v)_{K}=(u,{}_{i}e^{\prime}v)_{K}\text{ for $i\in I$, $u,v\in\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})$}.

We set
\mathbb{A}=\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
and let

\mathcal{U}_{\mathbb{A}}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
be the
\mathbb{A}
-subalgebra of

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
generated by
e_{i}^{(n)}\mathbin{:=}e_{i}^{n}/[n]_{i}!

for
i\in I
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{>0}
.

Let
\Delta_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the algebra homomorphism
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})\mathop{\otimes}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
given by
\Delta_{\mathfrak{n}}(e_{i})=e_{i}\mathop{\otimes}1+1\mathop{\otimes}e_{i}
,
where the algebra structure on
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})\mathop{\otimes}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
is defined by

(x_{1}\mathop{\otimes}x_{2})\cdot(y_{1}\mathop{\otimes}y_{2})=q^{-({\rm wt}(x_{2}),{\rm wt}(y_{1}))}(x_{1}y_{1}\mathop{\otimes}x_{2}y_{2}).

Set

\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})=\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{\beta\in\mathsf{Q}^{-}}\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{\beta}\quad\text{ where }\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{\beta}\mathbin{:=}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})}(\mathcal{U}^{+}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})_{-\beta},\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})).

Then
\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
is an algebra with the multiplication given by

(\psi\cdot\theta)(x)=\theta(x_{(1)})\psi(x_{(2)})
, when

\Delta_{\mathfrak{n}}(x)=x_{(1)}\mathop{\otimes}x_{(2)}
in Sweedler’s notation.
Let us denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
the
\mathbb{A}
-submodule
of
\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})

consisting of
\uppsi\in\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
such that

\uppsi\left(\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathbb{A}}\right)\subset\mathbb{A}
. Then
it is an
\mathbb{A}
-subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
.

It is known
[
GLS13
,
Kas91
,
Lus93
,
Kim12
]
that

(4.3)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})\simeq\mathcal{U}_{\mathbb{A}}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})\simeq\mathbb{C}[N],

where
\mathbb{C}[N]
denotes the coordinate (commutative) algebra of the complex unipotent algebraic group
N
whose Lie algebra is
\mathfrak{n}
. Thus we have a natural algebra (surjective) homomorphism
{\operatorname{ev}}_{q=1}:\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})\to\mathbb{C}[N]
.
We call
\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
the
unipotent quantum coordinate algebra
.

For a dominant weight
\lambda\in\mathsf{P}^{+}
, let
V(\lambda)
be the
irreducible highest weight
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
-module with highest weight
vector
u_{\lambda}
of weight
\lambda
. Let
(\ ,\ )_{\lambda}
be the
non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form on
V(\lambda)
such that

(u_{\lambda},u_{\lambda})_{\lambda}=1
and
(xu,v)_{\lambda}=(u,\upvarphi(x)v)_{\lambda}
for
u,v\in V(\lambda)
and
x\in\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
, where
\upvarphi
is the algebra
antiautomorphism on
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
defined by
\upvarphi(e_{i})=f_{i}
,

\upvarphi(f_{i})=e_{i}
and
\upvarphi(q^{h})=q^{h}
. For each
\mu,\zeta\in\mathsf{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}\lambda
, the
unipotent quantum minor

D(\mu,\zeta)
is
an element in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
given by

(D(\mu,\zeta),x)_{K}=(xu_{\mu},u_{\zeta})_{\lambda}

for
x\in\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathbb{A}}
, where
u_{\mu}
and
u_{\zeta}
are the
extremal weight vectors in
V(\lambda)
of weight
\mu
and
\zeta
,
respectively.
Note that

{\rm wt}(D(\mu,\zeta))=\mu-\zeta
if
\mu-\zeta\in\mathsf{Q}^{-}
and
D(\mu,\zeta)
vanishes otherwise.

Definition 4.1
.

We set

(4.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(\mu,\zeta)\mathbin{:=}q^{-(\mu-\zeta,\mu-\zeta)/4+(\mu-\zeta,\rho)/2}D(\mu,\zeta)

and call it a
normalized quantum unipotent minor
.

Proposition 4.2

(
[
BZ05
,
GLS13
,
KKKO18
]
)
.

(1)

For
\lambda,\mu\in\mathsf{P}^{+}
and
s,t,s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\in\mathsf{W}
such that

(i)

\ell(ss^{\prime})=\ell(s)+\ell(s^{\prime})
and
\ell(tt^{\prime})=\ell(t)+\ell(t^{\prime})
,

(ii)

s^{\prime}s\lambda\preccurlyeq t^{\prime}\lambda
and
s^{\prime}\mu\preccurlyeq t^{\prime}t\mu
,

we have
\theta\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
such that

\widetilde{D}(s^{\prime}\mu,t^{\prime}t\mu)\widetilde{D}(s^{\prime}s\lambda,t^{\prime}\lambda)=q^{\theta}\widetilde{D}(s^{\prime}s\lambda,t^{\prime}\lambda)D(s^{\prime}\mu,t^{\prime}t\mu).

(2)

For
u,v\in\mathsf{W}
and
i\in I
satisfying
u<us_{i}
and
v<vs_{i}
, we have
\widetilde{\kappa},\widetilde{\zeta}\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
such that

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(u\varpi_{i},v\varpi_{i})\widetilde{D}(us_{i}\varpi_{i},vs_{i}\varpi_{i})=q^{\widetilde{\kappa}}\widetilde{D}(us_{i}\varpi_{i},v\varpi_{i})\widetilde{D}(u\varpi_{i},vs_{i}\varpi_{i})+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}}\prod_{j\sim i}^{\to}\widetilde{D}(u\varpi_{j},v\varpi_{j})^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}.

We call (
4.5
) the
determinantal identity
among unipotent quantum minors.

4.2.
Quantum cluster algebra structure

In this subsection, we briefly recall the quantum cluster algebra structure of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
investigated in
[
GLS13
,
GY17
]
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\sqcup\{\infty\}
.
For a sequence of indices
\textbf{{i}}=(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{r})\in I^{r}
,
1\leqslant k\leqslant r
and
j\in I
, we use the following notations:

(4.6)

\displaystyle w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{\leqslant k}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{k}},\quad\lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k}\mathbin{:=}w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{\leqslant k}\varpi_{i_{k}},\quad w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{\leqslant 0}=\mathrm{id},

\displaystyle k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}(j)

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\min(\{k<u\leqslant r\ |\ i_{u}=j\}\cup\{r+1\}),

\displaystyle k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}(j)

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\max(\{1\leqslant u<k\ |\ i_{u}=j\}\cup\{0\}),

\displaystyle k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{\pm}(j)\ \ \text{ if }i_{k}=j.

If there is no danger of confusion, we drop the scripts
{}_{\textbf{{i}}}
or
{}^{\textbf{{i}}}
for notational simplicity.

Let
\textbf{{i}}=(i_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
be a sequence of
I
satisfying the following condition:

(4.7)

for any
i\in I
, we have
|\{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\ |\ i_{u}=i\}|=\infty.

For instance, when we take
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell})\in I(w_{\circ})
, we can extend it to
\widetilde{\textbf{{i}}}_{\circ}\in I^{\mathbb{N}}\mspace{1mu}
satisfying
(
4.7
) as follows:

(4.8)

\displaystyle i_{s+\ell}=i^{*}_{s}\text{ for }s\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}.

In particular, in this case, one can easily see that any successive subsequence of length
\leqslant\ell
of
\widetilde{\textbf{{i}}}_{\circ}
is reduced.

For
\textbf{{i}}=(i_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
satisfying (
4.7
), define an
\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\times\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
-matrix
\widetilde{B}_{\textbf{{i}}}=(b_{ab})_{a,b\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
and a symmetric
\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\times\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
-matrix
\Lambda_{\textbf{{i}}}=(\lambda_{st})_{s,t\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
as follows:

(4.9)

\displaystyle b_{ab}=\begin{cases}\pm 1&\text{ if }b=a^{\pm},\\
\mathsf{c}_{i_{a},i_{b}}&\text{ if }a<b<a^{+}<b^{+},\\
-\mathsf{c}_{i_{a},i_{b}}&\text{ if }b<a<b^{+}<a^{+},\\
0&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \lambda_{st}=(\varpi_{i_{s}}-w_{\leqslant s}\varpi_{i_{s}},\varpi_{i_{t}}+w_{\leqslant t}\varpi_{i_{t}})\ \ \text{ for }s<t.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
, we set

\mathsf{K}\mathbin{:=}\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}\ |\ 1\leqslant k\leqslant n\},\quad\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}}=\{k\in\mathsf{K}\ |\ k^{+}>n\},\quad\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}\mathbin{:=}\mathsf{K}\setminus\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}},

and define
\widetilde{B}^{n}_{\textbf{{i}}}\mathbin{:=}(b_{u,v})_{u\in\mathsf{K},v\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}}
and
\Lambda^{n}_{\textbf{{i}}}\mathbin{:=}(\lambda_{u,v})_{u,v\in\mathsf{K}}
.
We understand
\widetilde{B}^{\infty}_{\textbf{{i}}}=\widetilde{B}_{\textbf{{i}}}
and
\Lambda^{\infty}_{\textbf{{i}}}=\Lambda_{\textbf{{i}}}
with
\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}}=\emptyset
.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
\mathsf{K}
be a countable index set with a decomposition
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}\sqcup\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}}
. The indices in
\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}
(resp,
\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}}
) are called
exchangeable
(resp.
frozen
) indices.
A
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-valued
\mathsf{K}\times\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}
-matrix
\widetilde{B}=(b_{i,j})_{i\in\mathsf{K},j\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}}
is called an
exchange matrix
if

(a)

for each
j\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}
, there are finite many
i\in\mathsf{K}
such that
b_{i,j}\neq 0
,

(b)

its
\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}\times\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}
-submatrix
B\mathbin{:=}(b_{i,j})_{i,j\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}}
is
skew-symmetrizable
; i.e., there exists a sequence
S=(t_{i}\ |\ i\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}},\ t_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 1})
such that
t_{i}b_{i,j}=t_{j}b_{j,i}
for all
i,j\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}
.

Proposition 4.4

(
[
FHOO23
]
)
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\sqcup\{\infty\}
, the matrix
\widetilde{B}^{n}_{\textbf{{i}}}
is an exchange matrix and

\sum_{k\in\mathsf{K}}b_{k,u}\Lambda_{k,v}=2d_{i_{u}}\delta_{u,v}\quad\text{ for }u\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}},\ v\in\mathsf{K}.

In this case, the pair
(\Lambda^{n}_{\textbf{{i}}},\widetilde{B}^{n}_{\textbf{{i}}})
is called compatible.

Recall the notation
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{k}=w^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{\leqslant k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i_{k}})
for
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}\in I(w_{\circ})
and
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
. For
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}
with
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{k}={\mspace{1mu}\beta}

(1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell)
, we define

\displaystyle{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{+}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\begin{cases}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}^{+}}&\text{ if }k_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}^{+}\leqslant\ell,\\
0&\text{ if }k_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}^{+}=\ell+1,\end{cases}\qquad{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}\mathbin{:=}\begin{cases}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}^{-}}&\text{ if }k_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}^{-}>0,\\
0&\text{ if }k_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}^{-}=0,\end{cases}\qquad\lambda^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}\mathbin{:=}w^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{\leqslant k}\varpi_{i_{k}}.

We also define
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{(\pm 2)}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=({\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}})^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}
,
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{(\pm 3)}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=(({\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}})^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}})^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}
,
\dots
; that is,
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{(\pm k)}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\hskip-12.91663pt\underbrace{(\cdots(}_{\text{ $(k-1)$-braces}}\mkern-10.0mu\hskip-9.47217pt{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}})^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}\cdots)^{\pm}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}
.
If there is no danger of confusion, we also drop the scripts
{}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}
or
{}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}
for notational simplicity.

Remark 4.5
.

For
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}\in I(w_{\circ})
and its extension
i
via (
4.8
), let us set
(\Lambda_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}},\widetilde{B}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}})\mathbin{:=}(\Lambda^{\ell}_{\textbf{{i}}},\widetilde{B}^{\ell}_{\textbf{{i}}})
.
As
\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})

({\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+})
is well-defined, the assignment
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\mapsto\lambda_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}
does depend only on
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
. Thus, for
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}_{\circ}\overset{c}{\sim}\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}
,
we can obtain
(\Lambda_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}_{\circ}},\widetilde{B}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}_{\circ}})
by permuting the indices of
(\Lambda_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}},\widetilde{B}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}})
. Hence we can define a compatible pair
(\Lambda_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]},\widetilde{B}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]})
as follows:
Set
\mathsf{K}=\Phi_{+}
,
\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}}=\{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}\ |\ {\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{+}=0\}
and
\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}=\mathsf{K}\setminus\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{fr}}
. Define an
\mathsf{K}\times\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}
-matrix
\widetilde{B}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}=(b_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}})_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\mathsf{K},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}}
and
a symmetric
\mathsf{K}\times\mathsf{K}
-matrix
\Lambda_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}=(\lambda_{\eta\theta})_{\eta,\theta\in\mathsf{K}}
by setting

\displaystyle b_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}=\begin{cases}\pm 1&\text{ if }{\mspace{1mu}\beta}={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{\pm},\\
\mathsf{c}_{i_{a},i_{b}}&\text{ if }{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{+}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{+}\text{ and }i_{a}\sim i_{b},\\
-\mathsf{c}_{i_{a},i_{b}}&\text{ if }{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{+}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{+}\text{ and }i_{a}\sim i_{b},\\
0&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\quad\text{ where ${\mspace{1mu}\alpha}={\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{a}$ and ${\mspace{1mu}\beta}={\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{b}$,}

and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\eta\theta}=(\varpi_{i}-\lambda_{\eta},\varpi_{j}+\lambda_{\theta})\quad\text{ for }\ \theta\not\preccurlyeq_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}\eta\ \text{ with }\ i=\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\eta)\text{ and }j=\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\theta)

satisfying

\sum_{\gamma\in\mathsf{K}}b_{\gamma{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}\Lambda_{\gamma{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}=2d_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\delta_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}\quad\text{ for all ${\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{ex}}$ and ${\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\mathsf{K}$.}

We define the quantum torus
(\mathcal{T}_{q,[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]},\star)
as the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra generated by the set of generators
\{\widetilde{Y}^{\pm 1}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\ |\ {\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}\}
satisfying the relations

(4.10)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\star\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{-1}=\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{-1}\star\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}=1\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\star\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}=q^{\Lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}}\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\star\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}.

We define
{\rm wt}(\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\mathbin{:=}-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{(-k)}
.
Note that there exists a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-algebra anti-involution on
\mathcal{T}_{q,[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}
sending

(4.11)

\displaystyle q^{\pm 1/2}\longmapsto q^{\mp 1/2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\longmapsto\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\quad\text{ for all }{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}

(cf. (
2.7
)).

Let
\mathscr{A}(\Lambda_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]},\widetilde{B}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]})
be the quantum cluster algebra associated with
(\Lambda_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]},\widetilde{B}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]})
. It is a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-subalgebra of
\mathcal{T}_{q,[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}

generated by quantum cluster variables (we refer to
[
FHOO23
, Appendix A]
for the quantum cluster algebra and related notions).

For
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}
with
\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})=\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=i
, we write

\widetilde{D}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{D}(\lambda^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},\lambda^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}),\quad\widetilde{D}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{D}(\lambda^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},\varpi_{i}).

We understand
\widetilde{D}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})=0
unless
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\prec_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}
and
\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})=\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
,
and set
\widetilde{D}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(0,0)=1
.

The following theorem is proved in
[
GLS13
,
KKKO18
]
for simply-laced types and in
[
GY17
,
Qin20
]
for symmetrizable types.

Theorem 4.6

(
[
GLS13
,
GY17
,
KKKO18
,
Qin20
]
)
.

For a reduced sequence
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}
of
w_{\circ}
, there exists an
\mathbb{A}
-algebra isomorphism

{\rm CL}:\mathscr{A}(\Lambda_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]},\widetilde{B}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]})\to\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})

given by

\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\longmapsto\widetilde{D}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)\quad\text{ for }{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}.

4.3.
Dual PBW and dual-canonical/upper-global bases

Throughout this subsection, we fix
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
of
w_{\circ}
. For
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}
, we set

F^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\mathbin{:=}D_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{-})\quad\left(\text{resp. }\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{D}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{-})\right)

and call

\left\{\left.\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\ \right|\ {\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}\right\}\quad\left(\text{resp. }\left\{\left.\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\ \right|\ {\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}\right\}\right)

the set of
(
resp. normalized
)
dual PBW-vectors associated with
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
(cf.
[
GLS13
, Proposition 7.4]
).

For an exponent
\underline{\textbf{{e}}}=(\textbf{{e}}_{\mspace{1mu}\beta})_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}^{\Phi_{+}}
, we set

\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\mathbin{:=}q^{-\sum_{k<l}\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k}}\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{l}}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{l})}\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{\ell})^{\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{\ell}}}\cdots\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{2})^{\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{2}}}\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{1})^{\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{1}}},

where
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k}\mathbin{:=}{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

(
See, for instance,
[
Lus93
, Chapter 40, 41]
.)
The set
\{\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\ |\ \underline{\textbf{{e}}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}^{\Phi_{+}}\}
forms a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-basis of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
.

The basis
\{\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\ |\ \underline{\textbf{{e}}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}^{\ell}\}
is referred to as a
normalized dual Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt type basis
.

Theorem 4.8

(
[
BKM14
]
)
.

For an
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
-minimal
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
-pair
{\underline{\textbf{{p}}}}=\langle\hskip-2.58334pt\langle{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\rangle\hskip-2.58334pt\rangle
for

\gamma={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}+{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}\setminus\Pi

(
defined in Section
2.3
)
, we have

(4.12)

\displaystyle F^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})F^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})-q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})}F^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})F^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})=q^{-p_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}}(1-q^{2(p_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}))})F^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\gamma)

(4.13)

\displaystyle\iff\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})-q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})}\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})=q^{-p_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}+({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})/2}(1-q^{2(p_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}))})\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\gamma).

In particular, when
p_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}=0
, we have

(4.14)

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})/2}\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})-q^{({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})/2}\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})}{(q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})}-q^{({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})})}=\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\gamma).

For a positive root
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}
, recall
d_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha})/2\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
.
Let
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}
be positive roots such that
\gamma={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}+{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}
. Then we have

(4.15)

\displaystyle p_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}=\begin{cases}2&\text{ if }d_{\gamma}=3\text{ and }d_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}=d_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}=1,\\
1&\text{ if }d_{\gamma}=2\text{ and }d_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}=d_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}=1,\\
1&\text{ if $\mathfrak{g}$ is of type $G_{2}$ and }d_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}=d_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}=d_{\gamma}=1,\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Note that there exists the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-algebra anti-involution, denoted also by
\overline{(\cdot)}
, on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
defined by

q^{\pm 1/2}\longmapsto q^{\mp 1/2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\longmapsto\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})\quad\text{ for all }{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+},

which satisfies (see
[
HO19
, (8.3)]
and
[
FHOO22
, §7.4]
)

(4.16)

\displaystyle\overline{(\cdot)}\circ{\rm CL}={\rm CL}\circ\overline{(\cdot)}.

Lusztig
[
Lus90a
,
Lus91
]
and Kashiwara
[
Kas91
]
have constructed a specific
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-basis
\mathbf{B}^{\rm up}
of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
, which is called the

dual-canonical/upper-global basis
.
In this paper, we consider the normalized one
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
of
\mathbf{B}^{\rm up}
.
Note that
\widetilde{D}(\mu,\zeta)\in\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
for
\mu\preccurlyeq\zeta
.

Theorem 4.9

(
[
Kim12
, Theorem 4.29]
, see also
[
KKK15
, Proposition 4.1.1]
)
.

For each
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
of
w_{\circ}
, we have

\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}=\{\widetilde{G}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\ |\ \underline{\textbf{{e}}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}^{\Phi_{+}}\},

where
\widetilde{G}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})
is the unique element of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
satisfying the following properties for each
\underline{\textbf{{e}}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}^{\Phi_{+}}
:

(4.17)

(a)

\overline{\widetilde{G}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}=\widetilde{G}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})
,

(b)

\widetilde{G}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})-\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\in\sum_{\underline{\textbf{{e}}}^{\prime}\widetilde{<}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}\underline{\textbf{{e}}}}q\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q]\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}^{\prime})
.

Here
\underline{\textbf{{e}}}^{\prime}=(\textbf{{e}}^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}})_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}}\widetilde{<}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}\underline{\textbf{{e}}}=(\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}})_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}}
means that there exists
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi^{+}
such that
\textbf{{e}}^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}<\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}
and
\textbf{{e}}^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}=\textbf{{e}}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}
for all
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\not\preccurlyeq_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}
.

5.
HLO isomorphisms

In this section, we shall prove that there exists an algebra isomorphism from
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
to
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}

sending
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
to
\mathsf{L}_{q,Q}
for a Dynkin quiver
Q
of type
\mathfrak{g}
. This isomorphism is sometimes referred to as the
HLO isomorphism
since it is established by Hernandez–Leclerc–Oya in
[
HL15
,
HO19
]
(see also
[
FHOO22
,
FHOO23
]
).

Recall the quantum torus
\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}
associated with a Dynkin quiver
Q
in Definition
3.21
.

Theorem 5.1
.

(
[
HL15
, Proposition 3.8]
,
[
KO23a
, Theorem 6.14]
)
For each Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
, there exists an isomorphism between quantum tori

(5.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Uppsi_{Q}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 16.53232pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.36401pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.2822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.2822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-16.53232pt\raise-11.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{Y}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.53232pt\raise-11.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.02121pt\raise-11.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.02121pt\raise-11.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{m^{(i)}[p,\xi_{i}]}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

for each
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}
with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
.

Recall Remark
3.23
and that

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})\subset\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}\simeq\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}\subset\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}.

The theorem below for simply-laced
\mathfrak{g}
is proved in
[
HL15
]
(see also
[
FHOO22
]
) and can be proved in our case through an argument similar to that of

[
FHOO22
, Theorem 8.6]
. Here we repeat the proof in order to show how to use the quantum folded
T
-system.

Theorem 5.2
.

The isomorphism
\Uppsi_{Q}
in (
5.1
) induces an isomorphism between
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
and
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}
, which sends the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-basis

\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
to the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-basis
\mathsf{L}_{q,Q}^{T}
of
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}
. More precisely, for each
\underline{\textbf{{e}}}\in\bm{(}{\textbf{{e}}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}}\bm{)}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}^{\Phi^{+}}
, we have

\Uppsi_{Q}\left(\widetilde{G}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\right)=L_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))_{\leqslant\xi},

where

m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}
is given by

u_{i,p}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))=\textbf{{e}}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\quad\text{ when }\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)\text{ for each $(i,p)\in\Gamma_{0}^{Q}$.}

Proof.

Let

\mathcal{S}_{Q}\mathbin{:=}\{(i;p,u)\in I\times\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{2}\ |\ (i,p),(i,u)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0},\ \xi_{i^{*}}-\mathsf{h}<p\leqslant u\leqslant\xi_{i}+2\}

and choose a total ordering
\prec
on
\mathcal{S}_{Q}
satisfying

(i;p,u)\prec(i^{\prime};p^{\prime},u^{\prime})\quad\text{ if either {\rm(i)} }u>u^{\prime},\text{ or {\rm(ii)} }u=u^{\prime}\text{ and }p>p^{\prime}.

For each
(i;p,u)\in\mathcal{S}_{Q}
, we set

(5.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(i;p,u)\mathbin{:=}\begin{cases}\widetilde{D}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})&\text{ if }u\leqslant\xi_{i},\\
\widetilde{D}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)&\text{ if }u=\xi_{i}+2,\\
1&\text{ if }p=u,\end{cases}

where
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
and
\phi_{Q}(i,u)=({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)
.
Note that (
4.5
) can be expressed as

(5.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(i;p,u)\widetilde{D}(i;p^{+},u^{+})=q^{\widetilde{\kappa}}\widetilde{D}(i;p,u^{+})\widetilde{D}(i;p^{+},u)+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}}\prod_{j\sim i}\widetilde{D}(j;p^{+}(j),u^{+}(j))^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}},

where
t^{\pm}=t\pm 2
and
t^{+}(j)=\min\{t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}\ |\ (j,t^{\prime})\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0},\;t<t^{\prime}\}
for
t\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.

To show
\Uppsi_{Q}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n}))\simeq\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}
, it is enough to show that

(5.4)

\displaystyle\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(i;p,u))=F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p,u-2])_{\leqslant\xi}

for each
(i;p,u)\in\mathcal{S}_{Q}
, by Proposition
4.7
. Let us apply an induction with respect to
\prec
on
\mathcal{S}_{Q}
. When
u=\xi_{i}+2
, we have

\displaystyle\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(i;p,u))=\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0))

\displaystyle=\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{\mspace{1mu}\alpha})=\underline{m^{(i)}[p,\xi_{i}]}=F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p,\xi_{i}])_{\leqslant\xi}.

Since the case
p=u
is trivial, we consider the case when
p<u\leqslant\xi_{i}
.

Denoting by
\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]})
(resp.
\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{X}_{q,Q})
) the skew-field of fractions of
\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]}
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}
), one can extend the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-algebra anti-involution
\overline{(\cdot)}
of
\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]}
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{q,Q}
) to the

\mathbb{Q}
-algebra anti-involution on
\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]})
(resp.
\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{X}_{q,Q})
). Then we have
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra isomorphism
\widetilde{\Uppsi}_{Q}:\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]})\to\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{X}_{q,Q})
satisfying

\widetilde{\Uppsi}_{Q}\circ\overline{(\cdot)}=\overline{(\cdot)}\circ\widetilde{\Uppsi}_{Q}.

From (
5.3
),
\widetilde{D}(i;p,u)
is an element
in
\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{T}_{q,[Q]})
that is
\overline{(\cdot)}
-invariant and of the form

(5.5)

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{\widetilde{\kappa}}\widetilde{D}(i;p,u^{+})\widetilde{D}(i;p^{+},u)+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}}\prod_{j\sim i}\widetilde{D}(j;p^{+}(j),u^{+}(j))^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}}{\widetilde{D}(i;p^{+},u^{+})}.

By applying the induction hypothesis, we see that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uppsi}_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(i;p,u))

\displaystyle\ \ =\dfrac{\left(q^{\widetilde{\kappa}}F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p,u])_{\leqslant\xi}*F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p+2,u-2])_{\leqslant\xi}+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}}\prod_{j\sim i}F_{q}(m^{(j)}[p+1,u+1])_{\leqslant\xi}^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}\right)}{F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p+2,u])_{\leqslant\xi}}

is a
\overline{(\cdot)}
-invariant element. On the other hand, Theorem
3.19
tells us that

F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p,u-2])_{\leqslant\xi}
is the
\overline{(\cdot)}
-invariant element of the form

\displaystyle\dfrac{\left(q^{\kappa}F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p,u])_{\leqslant\xi}*F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p+2,u-2])_{\leqslant\xi}+q^{\zeta}\prod_{j\sim i}F_{q}(m^{(j)}[p+1,u+1])_{\leqslant\xi}^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}\right)}{F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p+2,u])_{\leqslant\xi}},

obtained from applying
(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}
on (
3.11
).
Since the
\overline{(\cdot)}
-invariance determines the values of
\kappa
and
\xi
uniquely,
the first assertion follows.

Now let us consider the second assertion. Since
\Uppsi_{Q}
commutes with
\overline{(\cdot)}
, the element
\Uppsi(\widetilde{G}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))
is
\overline{(\cdot)}
-invariant. By Theorem
2.8
and Theorem
4.9
(see also Remark
3.23
),
it is enough to show that

\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))=E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))_{\leqslant\xi}

for every
\textbf{{c}}\in(\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0})^{\ell}
. Note that we have already shown that

\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}))=\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{-}))=F_{q}(X_{i,p})_{\leqslant\xi}

as the special case of the first assertion, where
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
. Since
\Uppsi_{Q}
is a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra homomorphism, we have

\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))=q^{a(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))_{\leqslant\xi}\quad\text{ for some }a(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}.

To complete a proof of the second assertion, we need to show
a(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})=0
. Since
L_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))
and
\widetilde{G}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})
are
\overline{(\cdot)}
-invariant, the equations (
ii
) in (
2.11
) and (
b
) in (
4.17
)
can be rephrased in terms of
E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))
and
\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\overline{E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))}\in E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))+\sum_{m^{\prime}\prec_{\mspace{-2mu}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{\scalebox{.6}{${\rm N}$}}}m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1}]E_{q}(m^{\prime})\ \ \text{and}\ \ \overline{\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}\in\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})+\sum_{\underline{\textbf{{e}}}^{\prime}\widetilde{<}_{[Q]}\underline{\textbf{{e}}}}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1}]\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}^{\prime}).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))}

\displaystyle=\Uppsi_{Q}\left(\,\overline{\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}\,\right)=q^{a(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))_{\leqslant\xi}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\neq m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]E_{q}(m^{\prime})_{\leqslant\xi},

\displaystyle\overline{\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{F}^{\rm up}_{[Q]}(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))}

\displaystyle=\overline{q^{a(\textbf{{c}})}E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))_{\leqslant\xi}}=q^{-a(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}E_{q}(m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}}))_{\leqslant\xi}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\neq m(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]E_{q}(m^{\prime})_{\leqslant\xi}.

Thus
a(\underline{\textbf{{e}}})=0
and the second assertion also follows.
∎

Corollary 5.3
.

(
HLO isomorphism
)
For each Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
, there exists a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-algebra isomorphism

\Psi_{Q}:\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}

such that
(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}\circ\Psi_{Q}=\Uppsi_{Q}
. Furthermore, this isomorphism sends
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
to
\mathsf{L}_{q,Q}
.

Now we can give an affirmative answer to Conjecture
1
when
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}
.

Corollary 5.4
.

For a KR-monomial
m^{(i)}_{k,p}
with
m^{(i)}_{k,p}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}
for some Dynkin quiver
Q
, we have

F_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,p})=L_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,p}).

Proof.

By (
5.4
),
F_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,p})
corresponds to a normalized unipotent quantum minor, so it is contained in
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
. Thus it follows from Corollary
5.3
that
F_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,p})=L_{q}(m)
for some
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}
.
By Theorem
2.7
and Theorem
2.8
,
L_{q}(m)
should have
\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,p}
as its maximal
\mathcal{X}_{q}
-monomial in
L_{q}(m)
with respect to
\preccurlyeq_{\mspace{-2mu}\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\scalebox{.6}{${\rm N}$}}}
. Hence we conclude that
m=m^{(i)}_{k,p}
, which implies our assertion.
∎

The following corollary can be obtained from combining HLO isomorphism in Corollary
5.3
and the main result of Qin
[
Qin20
, Theorem 1.2.2]
(see also
[
McN21
]
), which proves that every cluster monomial of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
is contained in
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
.
Notice that this corollary also recovers Corollary
5.4
.

Corollary 5.5
.

Every cluster monomial of
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}
is contained in the canonical basis
\mathsf{L}_{q,Q}
.

Example 5.6
.

5.2
in type
C_{3}

Here
\widetilde{D}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{i},0)

(1\leqslant i\leqslant 3)
are the normalized dual PBW-vectors with weights
-{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{i}
, while

\widetilde{D}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{i},0)

(4\leqslant i\leqslant 9)
are not.

On the other hand, for
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k}\in\Phi_{+}
with
\mathrm{res}^{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{k})=i
, we have

(1)

i=1:

\lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{1}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{1}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},\ \ \lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{4}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{1}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},\ \ \lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{7}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{1}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},

(2)

i=2:

\lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{2}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{2}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},\ \ \lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{5}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{2}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},\ \ \lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{8}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{2}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-4{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},

(3)

i=3:

\lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{3}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{3}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},\ \ \lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{6}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{3}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-4{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},\ \ \lambda_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{9}}^{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}}=\varpi_{3}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}-4{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}-3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},

and each
\widetilde{D}(i;p,p+2)
is the normalized dual PBW-vector (see the convention in (
5.2
)):

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{F}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}^{\rm up}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{5})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern up}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{9})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 125.69092pt\raise-39.15527pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 152.9173pt\raise-49.83325pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{F}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}^{\rm up}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{6})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 294.59644pt\raise-39.15527pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 321.82281pt\raise-49.83325pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 379.0492pt\raise-49.83325pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{F}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}^{\rm up}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}_{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 463.50195pt\raise-39.15527pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}

In fact, (
5.4
) asserts that each generator
F_{q}(X_{i,p})_{\leqslant\xi}
of
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}
for
(i,p)\in\Gamma_{0}^{Q}
corresponds to the normalized dual PBW-vector
\widetilde{F}_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}^{\rm up}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})
with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
for
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}
under (
5.6
), which implies that
\Uppsi_{Q}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n}))\simeq\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}^{T}
because
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
is generated by the (normalized) dual PBW-vectors.

Now we explain the computations behind the proof of (
5.4
).
First, if
u=\xi_{i}+2
, then
\widetilde{D}(i;p,u)=\widetilde{D}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
for
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}
satisfying
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},0)
, so
\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(i;p,u))=F_{q}(m^{(i)}[p,\xi_{i}])_{\leqslant\xi}
by (
5.6
).
Second, let us determine
\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(1;1,3))
,
\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(2;0,2))
, and
\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(3;-1,1))
.
By (
5.3
), we have

(5.38)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(1;1,3)\widetilde{D}(1;3,5)=q^{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}\widetilde{D}(1;1,5)+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}}\widetilde{D}(2;2,4),

where
\widetilde{D}(1;1,5)\mapsto\underline{m^{(1)}[1,3]}
,
\widetilde{D}(2;2,4)\mapsto\underline{m^{(2)}[2,2]}
, and
\widetilde{D}(1;3,5)\mapsto\underline{m^{(1)}[3,3]}
under (
5.6
).
We remark that the half-integers
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}
could be computed explicitly by
[
GLS13
, Proposition 5.4]
.
This yields

(5.39)

\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(1;1,3))=\frac{q^{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}\underline{X_{1,1}X_{1,3}}+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}}\underline{X_{2,2}}}{\underline{X_{1,3}}}=q^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{1,1}+q^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{2,2}*\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{-1}=F_{q}(X_{1,1})_{\leqslant\xi}.

where
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
(cf.
[
JLO23
, Appendix A]
for the formula of
F_{q}(X_{1,1})
).
Note that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}
are not only determined by (
5.38
), but also by the bar-invariance of
\widetilde{D}(1;1,3)
and
F_{q}(X_{1,1})_{\leqslant\xi}
.
A similar computation gives

(5.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(2;0,2)\widetilde{D}(2;2,4)

\displaystyle=q^{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}\widetilde{D}(2;0,4)+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}_{2}}\widetilde{D}(1;1,3)\widetilde{D}(3;1,3),

(5.41)

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}(3;-1,1)\widetilde{D}(3;1,3)

\displaystyle=q^{\widetilde{\kappa}_{3}}\widetilde{D}(3;-1,3)+q^{\widetilde{\zeta}_{3}}\widetilde{D}(2;0,2)^{2},

where
\widetilde{D}(2;2,4)\mapsto\underline{m^{(2)}[2,2]}
,
\widetilde{D}(2;0,4)\mapsto\underline{m^{(2)}[0,2]}
,
\widetilde{D}(3;1,3)\mapsto\underline{m^{(3)}[1,1]}
,
\widetilde{D}(3;-1,3)\mapsto\underline{m^{(3)}[-1,1]}
,
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}=\frac{1}{2},\,\widetilde{\zeta}_{2}=\frac{3}{2}
, and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{3}=0,\,\widetilde{\zeta}_{3}=2
.
Note that (
5.38
), (
5.40
), and (
5.41
) correspond to the quantum folded
T
-system (
3.11
) by
\Uppsi_{Q}
.
By (
5.38
), (
5.39
), (
5.40
), and (
5.41
), we obtain

(5.42)

\displaystyle\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(2;0,2))=q^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{2,0}+q^{\frac{5}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{1,1}*\widetilde{X}_{3,1}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2}^{-1}+q^{\frac{3}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{3,1}*\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{-1}=F_{q}(X_{2,0})_{\leqslant\xi},

(5.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(3;-1,1))=q\widetilde{X}_{3,-1}+q^{2}\widetilde{X}_{2,0}^{2}*\widetilde{X}_{3,1}^{-1}+(1+q^{2})\widetilde{X}_{2,1}*\widetilde{X}_{1,2}*\widetilde{X}_{2,3}^{-1}+(q^{-1}+q)\widetilde{X}_{2,0}*\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{-1}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+q^{5}\widetilde{X}_{1,1}^{2}*\widetilde{X}_{3,1}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2}^{-1}+(q+q^{3})\widetilde{X}_{1,1}*\widetilde{X}_{3,1}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2}^{-1}*\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{-1}+q^{2}\widetilde{X}_{3,1}*\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{-2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad=F_{q}(X_{3,-1})_{\leqslant\xi}.\end{split}

Here (
5.39
) (resp. (
5.42
)) is used to compute (
5.42
) (resp. (
5.43
)).
Finally, one can see that

\displaystyle\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(1;-1,1))=F_{q}(X_{1,-1})_{\leqslant\xi},\quad\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(2;-2,0))=F_{q}(X_{2,-2})_{\leqslant\xi},\quad\Uppsi_{Q}(\widetilde{D}(3;-3,-1))=F_{q}(X_{3,-3})_{\leqslant\xi}

by the same procedure as in the second case.
We leave the details to the reader, and
refer to
[
JLO23
, Appendix A]
for the explicit formulas of
F_{q}(X_{1,p})
,
F_{q}(X_{2,p})
, and
F_{q}(X_{3,p})
for
p\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.

6.
Categorification and multiplicative relations

The aim of this section is to investigate the multiplicative relations for pairs of
L_{q}(X_{i,p})
’s, which is a preparation to prove the remaining main results.
Our main ingredient is the categorification of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
by graded
R
-modules
[
KL09
,
KL11
,
Rou08
,
KKKO18
]
, where
R
is the quiver Hecke algebra.
Combined with Theorem
5.2
, this yields the relationship (
6.1
) between the Grothendieck ring of graded
R
-modules and the heart subring
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}
of the quantum virtual Grothendieck ring
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
.
In particular, it allows us to utilize the crucial invariant (Definition
6.8
) closely related to the simplicity for the convolution product of certain simple
R
-modules
[
KKKO15
,
KKOP21b
]
, which is determined by the inverse of
\underline{\mathsf{B}}(t)

[
Fuj22
,
KO23b
]
.
When (
6.1
) is taken into account,
these results enable us to obtain the multiplicative relations for certain pairs of
L_{q}(X_{i,p})
’s.

6.1.
Quiver Hecke algebras and quantum virtual Grothendieck rings

Let
\mathbf{k}
be a field. For
i,j\in I
, we choose polynomials
\mathcal{Q}_{i,j}(u,v)\in\mathbf{k}[u,v]
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{i,j}(u,v)=\mathcal{Q}_{j,i}(v,u)
, which is of the form

\mathcal{Q}_{i,j}(u,v)=\delta(i\neq j)\displaystyle\sum_{pd_{i}+qd_{j}=-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j})}t_{i,j;p,q}u^{p}v^{p}\quad\text{ where }t_{i,j;-\mathsf{c}_{i,j},0}\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}.

For
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\mathsf{Q}^{+}
with
{\rm ht}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=m
, we set

I^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\mathbin{:=}\Bigl\{\eta=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m})\in I^{m}\ \bigm|\ \sum_{k=1}^{m}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{\eta_{k}}={\mspace{1mu}\beta}\Bigr\}.

The
quiver Hecke algebra

R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
associated with
\mathsf{C}
and
(\mathcal{Q}_{i,j}(u,v))_{i,j\in I}
is the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-graded
\mathbf{k}
-algebra generated by

\{e(\eta)\ |\ \eta\in I^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\},\ \{x_{k}\ |\ 1\leqslant k\leqslant m\},\ \{\tau_{l}\ |\ 1\leqslant l<m\}

subject to certain relations (see
[
KL09
,
KL11
,
Rou08
]
). Here
1=\sum_{\eta\in I^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}e(\eta)
, the elements

e(\eta)
’s are idempotents and
the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-grading of
R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
is defined by

\deg(e(\eta))=0,\quad\deg(x_{k}e(\eta))=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{\eta_{k}},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{\eta_{k}}),\quad\deg(\tau_{l}e(\eta))=-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{\eta_{l}},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{\eta_{l+1}}).

We denote by
R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
the category of finite-dimensional graded
R(\beta)
-modules with degree-preserving homomorphisms. For
M,N\in R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
, we denote by
\operatorname{Hom}_{R(\beta)}(M,N)
the space of degree-preserving module homomorphisms.
We set
R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}\mathbin{:=}\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathsf{Q}^{+}}R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
.
The trivial
R(0)
-module of degree 0 is denoted by
\mathbf{1}
.
We define the grading shift functor
q

by
(qM)_{k}=M_{k-1}
for a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
-graded module
M=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}}M_{k}
.

For
M,N\in R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
, we define

\mathrm{H{\scriptstyle OM}}_{R(\beta)}(M,N)\mathbin{:=}\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}}\operatorname{Hom}_{R(\beta)}(q^{k}M,N).

For
M\in R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
, we set
M^{\star}\mathbin{:=}\mathrm{H{\scriptstyle OM}}_{R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})}(M,R({\mspace{1mu}\beta}))
with the
R(\beta)
-action given by

(r\cdot f)(u)\mathbin{:=}f(\psi(r)u),\quad\text{for $f\in M^{\star}$, $r\in R(\beta)$ and $u\in M$,}

where
\psi
is the antiautomorphism of
R(\beta)
which fixes the generators.
We say that
M
is
self-dual
if
M\simeq M^{\star}
. For
M\in R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}
, we set
{\rm wt}(M)\mathbin{:=}-{\mspace{1mu}\beta}
.

For
{\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Q}^{+}
, we set

e(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\mathbin{:=}\sum_{\eta\in I^{\beta},\eta^{\prime}\in I^{\beta^{\prime}}}e(\eta,\eta^{\prime}),

where
e(\eta,\eta^{\prime})
is the idempotent corresponding to the concatenation

\eta\ast\eta^{\prime}
of

\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
.
It induces an injective ring homomorphism

R(\beta)\otimes R(\beta^{\prime})\to e(\beta,\beta^{\prime})R(\beta+\beta^{\prime})e(\beta,\beta^{\prime})

defined by

\displaystyle e(\eta)\otimes e(\eta^{\prime})\mapsto e(\eta,\eta^{\prime}),\quad x_{k}e(\beta)\otimes 1\mapsto x_{k}e(\beta,\beta^{\prime}),\quad 1\otimes x_{k}e(\beta^{\prime})\mapsto x_{k+{\rm ht}(\beta)}e(\beta,\beta^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\tau_{k}e(\beta)\otimes 1\mapsto\tau_{k}e(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\quad\text{ and }\quad 1\otimes\tau_{k}e(\beta^{\prime})\mapsto\tau_{k+{\rm ht}(\beta)}e(\beta,\beta^{\prime}).

For
M\in R(\beta)\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}
and
N\in R(\beta^{\prime})\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}
, we set

M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N\mathbin{:=}R(\beta+\beta^{\prime})e(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\otimes_{R(\beta)\otimes R(\beta^{\prime})}(M\otimes N).

We say that simple
R
-modules
M
and
N

strongly commute
if
M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N
is simple. A simple
R
-module

L
is said to be
real
if
L
strongly commutes with itself. Note that if
M
and
N
strongly commute, then
M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N\simeq q^{k}N\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}M
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.
We denote by
K(R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod})
the Grothendieck ring of
R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
induced by
\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}
. Note that

K(R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod})
has a natural
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1}]
-algebra structure given by grading shifts.

Theorem 6.1

(
[
KL09
,
KL11
,
Rou08
]
)
.

There exists a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra isomorphism

\Upomega:\mathcal{K}_{\mathbb{A}}(R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod})\mathbin{:=}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1}]}K(R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod})\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})

which preserves weights.

Proposition 6.2

(
[
KKOP18
, Proposition 4.1]
)
.

For
\varpi\in\mathsf{P}^{+}
and
\mu,\zeta\in\mathsf{W}\varpi
with
\mu\preccurlyeq\zeta
, there exists a self-dual real simple
R(\zeta-\mu)
-module
\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)
such that

\Upomega([\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)])=D(\mu,\zeta).

Here,
[\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)]
denotes the isomorphism class of
\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)
.

We call
[\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)]
the
determinantal module
associated to
D(\mu,\zeta)
.
For
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
of
w_{\circ}
and
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\in\Phi_{+}
, we write
S_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})
the determinantal
R({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})
-module such that

\Upomega([S_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})])=D_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{-}).

We call
S_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha})
the
cuspidal module
associated with
[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]
and
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}
. Note that
{\rm wt}(S_{[\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}))=-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}
.
For a Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
and
(i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}
, we set

S_{Q}(i;p)\mathbin{:=}S_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})\quad\text{ where }\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0).

Theorem 6.3
.

For each Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
,
we have the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra isomorphism
\Upxi_{Q}
given by

\Upxi_{Q}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 30.99327pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.3779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.82878pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 102.82878pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{\mathbb{A}}(R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-30.99327pt\raise-10.78668pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.99329pt\raise-10.78668pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.82652pt\raise-10.78668pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 56.82652pt\raise-10.78668pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{q^{({\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})/4+({\mspace{1mu}\beta},\rho)/2}[S_{Q}(i;p)]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where
(i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}
with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)
.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
5.2
, Corollary
5.3
, and Theorem
6.1
that
we have the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]
-algebra isomorphism
\Upxi_{Q}
given as follows:

(6.1)

\begin{split}\raisebox{47.00003pt}{ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 56.55222pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 82.38547pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 154.92009pt\raise-5.06664pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Upomega}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 188.96829pt\raise-15.52234pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.57303pt\raise-4.07776pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Psi_{Q}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.37794pt\raise-13.87598pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.36617pt\raise-28.02551pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13113pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Upxi_{Q}\mathbin{:=}\Omega\circ\Psi_{Q}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 188.96829pt\raise-20.91663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 96.00357pt\raise-20.91663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{q}(X_{i,p})=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 96.00357pt\raise-42.5227pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
