for
(i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}
with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)
.
Note that the last correspondence follows from

\Psi_{Q}^{-1}(L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0))=\widetilde{F}^{{\rm up}}_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=\Omega^{-1}(q^{({\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})/4+({\mspace{1mu}\beta},\rho)/2}[S_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})])

due to Theorem
5.2
and Proposition
6.2
.
∎

6.2.
Affreal modules, invariants, and multiplicative relations

For
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\mathsf{Q}^{+}
with
{\rm ht}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=m
and
i\in I
, let

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{i,{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}=\sum_{\eta\,\in I^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}\left(\prod_{a\in[1,m];\ \eta_{a}=i}x_{a}\right)e(\eta)\in Z(R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})),

where
Z(R({\mspace{1mu}\beta}))
denotes the center of
R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
.

Definition 6.4
.

Let
M
be an
R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
-module. We say that
M

admits an affinization
if there exists
R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
-module
\widehat{\mathsf{M}}

satisfying the followings: There exists an endomorphism
z_{\widehat{\mathsf{M}}}
of degree
t_{\widehat{\mathsf{M}}}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{>0}
such that
\widehat{\mathsf{M}}/z_{\widehat{\mathsf{M}}}\widehat{\mathsf{M}}\simeq M
and

(i)

\widehat{\mathsf{M}}
is a finitely generated free modules over the polynomial ring
\mathbf{k}[z_{\widehat{\mathsf{M}}}]
,

(ii)

\mathfrak{p}_{i,{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}\widehat{\mathsf{M}}\neq 0
for all
i\in I
.

We say that a simple
R({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
-module
M
is
affreal
if
M
is real and admits an affinization.

Remark 6.5
.

It is proved in
[
KP18
, Proposition 2.5]
that every affinization is
essentially even
; that means
t_{\widehat{\mathsf{M}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{>0}
. Thus we assume that every affinization has an even degree.

Theorem 6.6

(
[
KKOP21b
, Theorem 3.26]
)
.

For
\varpi\in\mathsf{P}^{+}
and
\mu,\zeta\in\mathsf{W}\varpi
with
\mu\preccurlyeq\zeta
, the determinantal module
\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)

admits an affinization
\widehat{\mathsf{M}}(\mu,\zeta)
. When
\varpi=\varpi_{i}
, the module
\mathsf{M}(\mu,\zeta)
admits an affinization of degree
({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i})=2d_{i}
.

Proposition 6.7

(
[
KKKO18
,
KKOP21b
]
)
.

Let
M
and
N
be simple modules such that one of them is affreal.
Then there exists a unique
R
-module homomorphism
\mathbf{r}_{M,N}\in\mathrm{H{\scriptstyle OM}}_{R}(M,N)
satisfying

\mathrm{H{\scriptstyle OM}}_{R}(M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N,N\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}M)=\mathbf{k}\,\mathbf{r}_{M,N}.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
M
and
N
be simple modules such that one of them is affreal.
We define

\displaystyle\Lambda(M,N)

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\deg(\mathbf{r}_{M,N}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Lambda}(M,N)

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\frac{1}{2}\big(\Lambda(M,N)+({\rm wt}(M),{\rm wt}(N))\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(M,N)

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\frac{1}{2}\big(\Lambda(M,N)+\Lambda(N,M)\big).

For
M,N\in R\text{-}\mathrm{gmod}
, let us
denote by
M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N
(resp.
M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}N
) the
head
(resp.
socle
) of
M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N
.

Proposition 6.9

(
[
KKKO15
, Theorem 3.2]
,
[
KP18
, Proposition 2.10]
,
[
KKOP21b
, Proposition 3.13, Lemma 3.17]
)
.

Let
M
and
N
be simple
R
-modules such that one of them is affreal.
Then we have

(i)

M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N
has simple socle and simple head,

(ii)

{\rm Im}(\mathbf{r}_{M,N})
is isomorphic to
M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N
and
N\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}M
,

(iii)

M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N
and
M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}N
appear once in the composition series of
M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N
, respectively.

Note that Proposition
6.2
and Theorem
6.6
tell us that
\left\{\,S_{Q}(i;p)\,\left|\,(i,p)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}\,\right.\right\}
is a family of affreal modules.
Hence the results below become the main ingredients for our purpose from the viewpoint of Theorem
6.3
.

Theorem 6.10

(
[
KKKO15
,
KKOP21b
]
)
.

Let
M
and
N
be simple modules such that one of them is affreal. Then the following three conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\mathfrak{d}(M,N)=0
,

(ii)

M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N\simeq q^{-\Lambda(M,N)}N\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}M
,

(iii)

M\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}N
is simple.

In particular, we have
\mathfrak{d}(M,M)=0
whenever
M
is affreal.

Let us recall (
2.3
) and (
2.5
).
Then the following theorem was proved in
[
Fuj22
]
for simply-laced types, and it is generalized recently in
[
KO23b
]
.

Theorem 6.11

(
[
Fuj22
,
KO23b
]
)
.

Let
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
be a Dynkin quiver. Then we have

\mathfrak{d}(S_{Q}(i;p),S_{Q}(j;s))=\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j}[|p-s|]\quad\text{ for any }(i,p),(j,s)\in\Gamma^{Q}_{0}.

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j}(t)
is defined in (
2.3
).
In particular, we have

\mathfrak{d}(S_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}),S_{[Q]}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))=\max(d_{i},d_{j})\delta(i\sim j).

Proposition 6.12

(
[
FHOO22
,
KO23b
]
)
.

Let
a\neq b\in I
and
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
be a Dynkin quiver with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{a},0)
and
\phi_{Q}(j,s)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{b},0)
. Then we have

\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j^{*}}[\mathsf{h}-|p-s|]=0.

Proof.

The proof can be found in
[
KO23b
, Corollary 8.9]
(cf.
[
FHOO22
, Lemma 9.12]
).
∎

Now we are ready to present the multiplicative relations for pairs of
L_{q}(X_{i,p})
’s.

Corollary 6.13
.

For
(i,p),(j,s)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
, assume that there exists a Dynkin quiver
Q
such that
(i,p),(j,s)\in\Gamma_{0}^{Q}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j}[|p-s|]=0
.
Then we have

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*L_{q}(X_{j,s})=q^{\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s)}L_{q}(X_{j,s})*L_{q}(X_{i,p})

and hence
\Lambda(S_{Q}(i;p),S_{Q}(j;s))=-\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s).

Proof.

By Theorem
6.10
and Theorem
6.11
,

S_{Q}(i;p)\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}S_{Q}(j;s)\simeq q^{k}S_{Q}(j;s)\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}S_{Q}(j;s)
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.
By Theorem
6.3
, we have

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*L_{q}(X_{j,s})=q^{k}L_{q}(X_{j,s})*L_{q}(X_{i,p}).

Since an element of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
(and so
\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}
) is determined by its dominant monomials (cf.
[
JLO23
, Lemma 5.20 and Lemma 5.40]
),
the integer
k
must be
\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s)
by comparing the dominant monomials on both sides (recall Definition
2.4
),
as we desired.
Note that the last statement follows directly from Theorem
6.3
and Theorem
6.10
(
ii
).
∎

Corollary 6.14
.

Let
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
be a Dynkin quiver. Let
(i,p),(j,s)\in{\widehat{\triangle}}_{0}
such that
\xi_{i}\geqslant p\geqslant s>\xi_{j}
. Then we have

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*L_{q}(X_{j,s})=L_{q}(X_{j,s})*L_{q}(X_{i,p})

Proof.

Take a Dynkin quiver
Q^{\prime}
such that
(i,p),(j,s)\in\Gamma^{Q^{\prime}}_{0}
. Take
a\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}
and
b\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 1}

such that
p=\xi_{i}-2a
and
s=\xi_{j}+2b
.
Then we have

\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j}[p-s]=\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{{\underline{\mathsf{b}}}}_{i,j}(p-s-1)=\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{{\underline{\mathsf{b}}}}_{i,j}(\xi_{i}-\xi_{j}-1-2(a+b)).

Since
\xi_{i}-\xi_{j}-1<d(i,j)
, Lemma
2.2
(
1
) tells us that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j}(p-s)=0
.
By Corollary
6.13
, we have

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*L_{q}(X_{j,s})=q^{\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s)}L_{q}(X_{j,s})*L_{q}(X_{i,p})

Now we claim that
\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s)=0
. Note that

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s)

\displaystyle=\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{{\underline{\mathsf{b}}}}_{i,j}(\xi_{i}-\xi_{j}-2a-2b-1)+\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{{\underline{\mathsf{b}}}}_{i,j}(\xi_{i}-\xi_{j}-2a-2b+1).

Since the two terms in (
6.3
) vanish by Lemma
2.2
(
1
), the claim holds.
∎

Corollary 6.15
.

Let
a\neq b\in I
and
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
be a Dynkin quiver with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{a},0)
and
\phi_{Q}(j,s)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{b},0)
. Then we have

(6.4)

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*\mathfrak{D}_{q}^{\pm 1}(L_{q}(X_{j,s}))=q^{\mp\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j,s)}\mathfrak{D}^{\pm 1}(L_{q}(X_{j,s}))*L_{q}(X_{i,p}),

where
\mathfrak{D}_{q}
is in (
3.6
) and we understand
\mathfrak{D}_{q}^{+1}=\mathfrak{D}_{q}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.18
,
\mathfrak{D}_{q}^{\pm 1}(L_{q}(X_{j,s}))=L_{q}(X_{j^{*},s\pm\mathsf{h}})
. If
|s\pm\mathsf{h}-p|>\mathsf{h}
, our assertion follows from Proposition
3.16
. Thus it suffices to prove the assertion for

\text{{\rm(i)} $s\leqslant p$ and $\mathfrak{D}_{q}$},\quad\text{ or }\quad\text{{\rm(ii)} $s\geqslant p$ and $\mathfrak{D}_{q}^{-1}$. }

In case (i), the right hand side of (
6.4
) becomes

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*L_{q}(X_{j^{*},s+\mathsf{h}}).

By taking Dynkin quiver
Q^{\prime}
such that

(i,p),(j^{*},s+\mathsf{h})\in\Gamma_{0}^{Q^{\prime}}
,
it follows from Proposition
6.12
, Theorem
6.10
and Theorem
6.11
that

S_{Q^{\prime}}(i,p)\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}S_{Q^{\prime}}(j^{*},s+\mathsf{h})\simeq q^{k}S_{Q^{\prime}}(j^{*},s+\mathsf{h})\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}S_{Q^{\prime}}(i,p)
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.
By Theorem
6.3
with respect to
Q^{\prime}
, we have

L_{q}(X_{i,p})*L_{q}(X_{j^{*},s+\mathsf{h}})\simeq q^{\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(i,p;j^{*},\mathsf{h})}L_{q}(X_{j^{*},s+\mathsf{h}})*L_{q}(X_{i,p}).

Since
\phi_{Q}(j^{*},s+\mathsf{h})=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{b},-1)
, the assertion for (i) follows from Theorem
3.5
. Case (ii) is also proved in a similar way.
∎

Example 6.16
.

We continue Example
5.6
.
Let us recall that

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 306.23831pt\raise-49.83325pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[\widetilde{S}(3;1)]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 369.57169pt\raise-38.3885pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}

where
\widetilde{S}(i;p)=q^{({\mspace{1mu}\beta},{\mspace{1mu}\beta})/4+({\mspace{1mu}\beta},\rho)/2}S_{Q}(i;p)
with
\phi_{Q}(i,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)
. As shown already, this relationship enables us to obtain the multiplicative relations of
L_{q}(X_{i,p})
’s from those of
[\widetilde{S}(i;p)]
’s.
By Proposition
2.3
, we have

(6.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{1,1}(t)=t+t^{5},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{1,2}(t)=t^{2}+t^{4},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{1,3}(t)=2t^{3},\\
&\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{2,2}(t)=t+2t^{3}+t^{5},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{2,3}(t)=2t^{2}+2t^{4},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{3,3}(t)=2t+2t^{3}+2t^{5}.\end{split}

Note that the following computations can also be deduced directly from the explicit formulas of
L_{q}(X_{i,p})
’s in
[
JLO23
, (A.1)]
.

(i)

(i,p)=(1,3)
and
(j,s)=(2,2)
. By (
6.5
), we have

\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{1,2}[1]=0
and
\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(1,3;2,2)=-1
, so it follows from Corollary
6.13
that

L_{q}(X_{1,3})*L_{q}(X_{2,2})=q^{-1}L_{q}(X_{2,2})*L_{q}(X_{1,3}),

where the products on both sides have unique dominant monomials
\widetilde{X}_{1,3}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2}
and
q\widetilde{X}_{1,3}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2}
, respectively.

(ii)

(i,p)=(2,0)
and
(j,s)=(3,-1)
. By (
6.5
), we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{2,3}[1]=0
and
\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(2,0;3,-1)=-2
. Thus we have

L_{q}(X_{2,0})*L_{q}(X_{3,-1})=q^{-2}L_{q}(X_{3,-1})*L_{q}(X_{2,0}),

where the products on both sides have unique dominant monomials
q^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{3,-1}*\widetilde{X}_{2,0}
and
q^{\frac{3}{2}}\widetilde{X}_{3,-1}*\widetilde{X}_{2,0}
, respectively.

(iii)

(i,p)=(1,3)
and
(j,s)=(3,-3)
. By (
6.5
), we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{1,3}[6]=0
, so it follows from Corollary
6.13
that

L_{q}(X_{1,3})*L_{q}(X_{3,-3})=L_{q}(X_{3,-3})*L_{q}(X_{1,3}).

Note that
\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(1,3;3,-3)=0
.

We remark that
L_{q}(X_{i,p})
and
L_{q}(X_{j,s})
do not
q
-commute in the case of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{i,j}[|p-s|]\neq 0
.
For example, for
(i,p)=(2,2)
and
(j,s)=(2,-2)
, we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{d}}_{2,2}[3]=2\neq 0
.
On the other hand,
L_{q}(X_{2,2})*L_{q}(X_{2,-2})
(resp.
L_{q}(X_{2,-2})*L_{q}(X_{2,2})
) has dominant monomials

\displaystyle q^{2}\widetilde{X}_{2,-2}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2},\quad q\widetilde{X}_{1,-1}*\widetilde{X}_{1,1},\quad(q^{-1}+q)\widetilde{X}_{2,0},

(resp.
q\widetilde{X}_{2,-2}*\widetilde{X}_{2,2},\quad q^{2}\widetilde{X}_{1,-1}*\widetilde{X}_{1,1},\quad(1+q^{2})\widetilde{X}_{2,0}
).

Hence
L_{q}(X_{2,2})*L_{q}(X_{2,-2})\neq q^{k}L_{q}(X_{2,-2})*L_{q}(X_{2,2})
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.

7.
Presentations and automorphisms

In this section, we introduce automorphisms
\sigma_{i,Q}^{\pm 1}
of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
with respect to a Dynkin quiver
Q
with a source
i
.
For this purpose, we establish BCFG-analogues of
[
FHOO22
, Section 10]
by means of our previous results.
The analogues concern the presentation and automorphisms of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
.
In Section
8
, we will show that such automorphisms induce a braid group action on
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
.

By (
3.10
), the commutative monoid
\mathcal{M}_{+}
can be understood as a product of
\mathfrak{D}^{k}\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}

(k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu})
for each Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
.
Thus for each
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}
, (
3.10
) tells us that
m
has a unique factorization

(7.1)

\displaystyle m=\prod_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}}\mathfrak{D}^{k}(m_{k})

such that
m_{k}\in\mathcal{M}^{Q}_{+}
, where
m_{k}=1
for all but finitely many
k
.
Using Corollary
6.14
, the proposition below holds in the same way as
[
FHOO22
, Proposition 10.1]
.

Proposition 7.1
.

For
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}
, the element
E_{q}(m)
can be written as

E_{q}(m)=q^{\kappa(m,Q)}\prod^{\to}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}}\mathfrak{D}_{q}^{k}(E_{q}(m_{k}))\in\mathfrak{K}_{q},

where

\kappa(m,Q)\mathbin{:=}-\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{k<l}(-1)^{k+l}({\rm wt}_{Q}(m_{k}),{\rm wt}_{Q}(m_{l})).

7.1.
The presentation

In this subsection, we exhibit a presentation of the
localized quantum virtual Grothendieck ring
defined by

\mathbb{K}_{q}\mathbin{:=}\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]}\mathfrak{K}_{q}.

Note that we can extend
\overline{(\cdot)}
and
\mathfrak{D}_{q}
on
\mathbb{K}_{q}
.
For a Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
, we also set

\mathbb{K}_{q,Q}\mathbin{:=}\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm 1/2}]}\mathfrak{K}_{q,Q}.

From now on, let us fix a Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
and set, for each
(i,k)\in I\times\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k}\mathbin{:=}L_{q}(X_{\iota,p})\qquad\text{ where\,\,\, $\phi_{Q}(\iota,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},k)$. }

Note that
\mathfrak{D}_{q}^{l}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k})=\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k+l}
for
l\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.
From (
4.3
) and Corollary
5.3
, we have an algebra isomorphism

(7.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\Psi}_{Q}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.62486pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.62486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.62486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.62486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{K}_{q,Q},}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-7.65077pt\raise-9.7478pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{f_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.65077pt\raise-9.7478pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.90755pt\raise-9.7478pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.90755pt\raise-9.7478pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,0}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

This implies that the quantum Serre relations holds for
i\neq j\in I
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
:

(7.4)

\sum_{s=0}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{s}\left[\begin{matrix}1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\\
s\end{matrix}\right]_{q_{i}}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,m})^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-s}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,m}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,m})^{s}=0.

Furthermore, it follows from Proposition
3.15
, Proposition
3.16
, and Corollary
6.15
that the quantum Boson relations also hold:

(7.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,k+1}

\displaystyle=q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j})}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,k+1}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k}+(1-q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i})})\delta_{i,j},

(7.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,l}

\displaystyle=q^{(-1)^{k+l}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j})}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,l}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k},

for
i,j\in I
and
k,l\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
with
l>k+1
.

Let us denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
the
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
-algebra generated by
\{\mathsf{y}_{i,k}\ |\ (i,k)\in I\times\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}\}
subject to the following relations:

(7.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{s}\left[\begin{matrix}1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\\
s\end{matrix}\right]_{q_{i}}\mathsf{y}_{i,k}^{1-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-s}\mathsf{y}_{j,k}\mathsf{y}_{i,k}^{s}=0,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}_{i,k}\mathsf{y}_{j,k+1}=q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j})}\mathsf{y}_{j,k+1}\mathsf{y}_{i,k}+(1-q^{-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i})})\delta_{i,j},

(7.9)

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}_{i,k}\mathsf{y}_{j,l}=q^{(-1)^{k+l}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j})}\mathsf{y}_{j,l}\mathsf{y}_{i,k},

for
i,j\in I
and
k,l\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
with
l>k+1
.

Theorem 7.2
.

For each Dynkin quiver
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
, there exists an
algebra isomorphism

\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}:\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})\to\mathbb{K}_{q}

such that
\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\left(\mathsf{y}_{i,k}\right)=\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,k}
for
(i,k)\in I\times\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.

Proof.

The proof is almost identical with that of
[
FHOO22
, Theroem 10.4]
(cf.
[
HL15
, Theorem 7.3]
), where the above result is proved for simply-laced types.
∎

7.2.
Automorphisms of
\mathfrak{K}_{q}

For a Dynkin diagram
\triangle
, let
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
and
Q^{\prime}=(\triangle,\xi^{\prime})
be Dynkin quivers whose underlying graphs
are the same as
\triangle
. Then we have an automorphism

\widetilde{\Uptheta}(Q^{\prime},Q)
of
\mathbb{K}_{q}
given by

(7.10)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}(Q^{\prime},Q)\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}^{-1},

which is compatible with
\overline{(\cdot)}
and
\mathfrak{D}_{q}
.
Using the isomorphisms
\widetilde{\Psi}_{Q}
’s in (
7.3
) preserving
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
, we define a bijection

\psi_{0}:\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}\to\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q^{\prime}}

such that

\widetilde{\Psi}_{Q^{\prime},Q}(L_{q}(m))\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{\Psi}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ\widetilde{\Psi}_{Q}^{-1}(L_{q}(m))=L_{q}(\psi_{0}(m))

for
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}^{Q}
,
where
\widetilde{\Psi}_{Q^{\prime},Q}
coincides with the restriction of
\widetilde{\Uptheta}(Q^{\prime},Q)
to
\mathbb{K}_{q,Q}
.
The bijection
\psi_{0}
can be extended naturally to
\mathcal{M}_{+}
by the decomposition (
7.1
) of
m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}
, that is,

\psi(m)\mathbin{:=}\prod_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}}\mathfrak{D}^{k}(\psi_{0}(m_{k}))\qquad\text{ for all }m\in\mathcal{M}_{+}.

In particular, when

(a)

Q^{\prime}=s_{i}Q
for a Dynkin quiver
Q
with a source
i
, and

(b)

\phi_{Q}(k,p)=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)
,

we have

\widetilde{\Uptheta}(s_{i}Q,Q)(L_{q}(X_{k,p}))=\widetilde{\Uptheta}(s_{i}Q,Q)(L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m))\overset{\star}{=}L^{s_{i}Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)=L^{Q}_{q}(s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),m),

where
\overset{\star}{=}
holds due to Theorem
7.2
(recall (
3.5
)).

Theorem 7.3
.

The automorphism
\widetilde{\Uptheta}(Q^{\prime},Q)
sends the canonical basis
\mathsf{L}_{q}
to itself. Hence it induces an automorphism
on
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
.

Proof.

By using Theorem
2.8
,
one may prove our assertion following the proof of
[
FHOO22
, Theroem 10.6]
, where the above is proved for simply-laced types.
∎

7.3.
Description of the automorphisms

Now let us focus on the case when
Q^{\prime}=s_{i}Q
for a source
i
of
Q
and write

\sigma_{i,Q}\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{\Uptheta}(s_{i}Q,Q)|_{\mathfrak{K}_{q}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\sigma_{i,Q}^{-1}\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{\Uptheta}(Q,s_{i}Q)|_{\mathfrak{K}_{q}}.

To describe
\sigma_{i,Q}
(resp.
\sigma^{-1}_{i,Q}
), it suffices to consider the images of
\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,m}
under
\sigma_{i,Q}
(resp.
\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,m}
under
\sigma^{-1}_{i,Q}
).

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
Q
be a Dynkin quiver with a source
i
, and hence
s_{i}Q
is a Dynkin quiver with a sink
i
.
Then the automorphisms
\sigma_{i,Q}^{\pm 1}
can be described as follows
:

(7.11)

\displaystyle\sigma_{i,Q}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,m})=\begin{cases}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,m+\delta_{i,j}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}\geqslant 0,\\[4.30554pt]
\dfrac{\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{k}q_{i}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}/2-k}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,m})^{(k)}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,m}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{i,m})^{(-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-k)}\right)}{(q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1})^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}<0,\end{cases}

and

(7.12)

\displaystyle\sigma_{i,Q}^{-1}(\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,m})=\begin{cases}\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,m-\delta_{i,j}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}\geqslant 0,\\[4.30554pt]
\dfrac{\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{k}q_{i}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}/2-k}(\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{i,m})^{(-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-k)}\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,m}(\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{i,m})^{(k)}\right)}{(q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1})^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}<0,\end{cases}

where

(\mathsf{x}^{Q^{\prime}}_{i,m})^{(k)}\mathbin{:=}\dfrac{(\mathsf{x}^{Q^{\prime}}_{i,m})^{k}}{[k]_{i}!}\quad\text{ for any Dynkin quiver $Q^{\prime}$}.

Before we see the proof, let us recall the
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
-algebra automorphisms
T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1},T^{\prime}_{i,1}:\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
which are inverse to each other (see
[
Lus93
, Section 37.1.3]
and also
[
Sai94
]
):

\displaystyle T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1}(q^{h})=q^{s_{i}(h)},\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \qquad\qquad\ T^{\prime}_{i,1}(q^{h})=q^{s_{i}(h)},

\displaystyle T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1}(e_{i})=-f_{i}K_{i}^{-1},\ T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1}(f_{i})=-K_{i}e_{i},\quad T^{\prime}_{i,1}(e_{i})=-K_{i}f_{i},\ T^{\prime}_{i,1}(f_{i})=-e_{i}K_{i}^{-1},

\displaystyle T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1}(e_{j})=\sum_{r+s=-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{r}q_{i}^{r}e_{i}^{(s)}e_{j}e_{i}^{(r)},\quad\quad T^{\prime}_{i,1}(e_{j})=\sum_{r+s=-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{r}q_{i}^{r}e_{i}^{(r)}e_{j}e_{i}^{(s)}\ \ \quad\text{ for }i\neq j,

\displaystyle T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1}(f_{j})=\sum_{r+s=-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{r}q_{i}^{-r}f_{i}^{(r)}f_{j}f_{i}^{(s)},\quad T^{\prime}_{i,1}(f_{j})=\sum_{r+s=-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{r}q_{i}^{-r}f_{i}^{(s)}f_{j}f_{i}^{(r)}\quad\text{ for }i\neq j.

Remark 7.5
.

Note that when
i\neq j
, the action

\sigma_{i,Q}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,m})
(resp.
\sigma^{-1}_{i,Q}(\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,m})
) coincides with the
T^{\prime\prime}_{i,-1}
-operation (resp.
T^{\prime}_{i,1}
-operation) on
f_{j}

(i\neq j)
(
[
Lus90b
,
Sai94
,
Lus93
]
) up to a constant in
\mathbb{Q}[[q^{\pm 1/2}]]\setminus\{0\}
.
Note also that
the formulas (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) for ADE-cases and their proofs are announced in
[
KKOP21a
]
where a different approach is taken.
From now on, we write

T_{i}\mathbin{:=}T^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{i,-1}\quad\text{ and }\quad T_{i}^{-1}\mathbin{:=}T^{{}^{\prime}}_{i,1}

for notational simplicity.

Proof of
Proposition
7.4
.

By Theorem
7.2
and (
7.10
), we have

(7.13)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}(s_{i}Q,Q)(\mathsf{x}_{j,m}^{Q})=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{s_{i}Q}\circ\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}^{-1}(\mathsf{x}_{j,m}^{Q})=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{s_{i}Q}(\mathsf{y}_{j,m})=\mathsf{x}_{j,m}^{s_{i}Q},

which is equivalent to

(7.14)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}(s_{i}Q,Q)(L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m))=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)=L_{q}^{Q}(s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),m).

(cf. (
3.5
) and (
7.2
)).
In what follows, we shall investigate how
\mathsf{x}_{j,m}^{s_{i}Q}
can written in terms of
\mathsf{x}_{j,n}^{Q}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, and obtain (
7.11
) and (
7.12
).
Since
\widetilde{\Uptheta}(s_{i}Q,Q)
is compatible with
\mathfrak{D}_{q}
, it is enough to consider the case of
\sigma_{i,Q}(\mathsf{x}_{j,0}^{Q})=\mathsf{x}_{j,0}^{s_{i}Q}
for any
j\in I
.

In the case of (
7.11
),
the
i
-th simple root
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}
is a minimal element of
\Phi_{+}
with respect to
\prec_{[Q]}
.
The assertion for
i=j
follows from the rule (
3.3
) (
ii
).
Thus let us consider three cases when
i\neq j
as follows:

Case 1
. Assume
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=-1
. In this case
\langle\hskip-2.58334pt\langle{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}\rangle\hskip-2.58334pt\rangle
is a
[Q]
-minimal
[Q]
-pair for
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}
and
p_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}}=0
.
From the viewpoint of Corollary
5.3
, we interpret the commutation relation (
4.14
) as follows:

\displaystyle\dfrac{q_{i}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},0)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},0)-q_{i}^{\mathsf{c}_{i,j}/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},0)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},0)}{(q_{i}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}-q_{i}^{\mathsf{c}_{i,j}})}=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},0)=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},0)

By (
7.13
) and (
7.14
), we conclude that
\sigma_{i,Q}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,0})=\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,0}
is written as (
7.11
) in this case.

Case 2
. Assume
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=-2
. Note that, when
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
B_{n}
(resp.
C_{n}
),
\langle\hskip-2.58334pt\langle{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}\rangle\hskip-2.58334pt\rangle
(resp.
\langle\hskip-2.58334pt\langle{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}\rangle\hskip-2.58334pt\rangle
) is a
[Q]
-minimal [Q]-pair,
since it is the only pair for
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}
(resp.
2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}
).
When
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
F_{4}
, one can check that
\langle\hskip-2.58334pt\langle{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{3}\rangle\hskip-2.58334pt\rangle
is also a
[Q]
-minimal [Q]-pair for any Dynkin quiver with source
3
. Since the proofs are similar, we assume that
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}
. In this case, we have

p_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}}=1\qquad\text{ and }\qquad({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})=0.

From the viewpoint of Corollary
5.3
,
the commutation relation (
4.13
) tells us that

\dfrac{L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},0)-L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},0)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0)}{q-q^{-1}}=L_{q}^{Q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0)=L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0).

Since

L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0)=\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},0)-q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},0)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},0)}{(q^{2}-q^{-2})},

we have

\displaystyle L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})=L_{q}^{Q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})=

\displaystyle\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})^{*2}-(q+q^{-1})L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})+q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})^{*2}*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})},

where
L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})\mathbin{:=}L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},0)
.
Hence
\sigma_{n,Q}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{n-1,0})=\mathsf{x}^{s_{n}Q}_{n-1,0}
gives (
7.11
) in this case.

Case 3
. The case
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=-3
is taken care of in Appendix
A
.

In the case of (
7.12
), the
i
-simple root
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}
is a maximal element of
\Phi_{+}
with respect to
\prec_{[s_{i}Q]}
. Then we apply the similar arguments for showing (
7.11
) to (
7.12
), which implies that

\sigma_{i,Q}^{-1}(\mathsf{x}^{s_{i}Q}_{j,0})=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}(s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))=\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{j,0}.

Thus
\sigma_{i,Q}^{-1}
gives a description of
\widetilde{\Uptheta}(Q,s_{i}Q)
, which is the inverse of
\sigma_{i,Q}
.
∎

Corollary 7.6
.

For
i\in I
and
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\Phi_{+}
with
i\not\in{\rm supp}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
, we have

\sigma_{i,Q}\big(L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},m)\big)=L_{q}^{Q}(s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}),m)\quad\text{ and }\quad\sigma_{i,Q}^{-1}\big(L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},m)\big)=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}(s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}),m),

where
Q
is a Dynkin quiver with source
i
.

Proof.

Recall that
{\rm supp}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=\{i\in I\ |\ k_{i}\neq 0\}
for
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}=\sum_{i\in I}k_{i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}\in\mathsf{Q}^{+}
.
Our assertion follows directly from Theorem
4.8
and Proposition
7.4
(see
Case 1
–
Case 3
in the proof of Proposition
7.4
).
∎

8.
Braid group action

For each
\triangle
, the braid group
B(\triangle)
(or
Artin–Tits
group) is generated by
T^{\pm}_{i}

(i\in\triangle_{0})
satisfying

(8.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\underbrace{T^{\pm}_{i}T^{\pm}_{j}\cdots}_{\text{ $h_{i,j}$-times}}&=\underbrace{T^{\pm}_{j}T^{\pm}_{i}\cdots}_{\text{ $h_{i,j}$-times}}\qquad\text{for all $i\neq j\in\triangle_{0}$,}\end{split}

where
h_{i,j}
is defined in (
2.1
).
In this section, we shall prove that the automorphisms
\sigma_{i,Q}^{\pm 1}
induce a braid group action on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
and hence on
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
by Theorem
7.3
.

8.1.
Braid group symmetry on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})

By Theorem
7.2
and Proposition
7.4
, we define automorphisms
\textbf{{T}}_{i}^{\pm 1}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})

by the following:

(8.2)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{j,m})=\begin{cases}\mathsf{y}_{j,m+\delta_{i,j}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}\geqslant 0,\\[4.30554pt]
\dfrac{\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{k}q_{i}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}/2-k}\mathsf{y}_{i,m}^{(k)}\mathsf{y}_{j,m}\mathsf{y}_{i,m}^{(-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-k)}\right)}{(q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1})^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}<0,\end{cases}

and

(8.3)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{i}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{j,m})=\begin{cases}\mathsf{y}_{j,m-\delta_{i,j}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}\geqslant 0,\\[4.30554pt]
\dfrac{\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}(-1)^{k}q_{i}^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}/2-k}\mathsf{y}_{i,m}^{(-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}-k)}\mathsf{y}_{j,m}\mathsf{y}_{i,m}^{(k)}\right)}{(q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1})^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}}}&\text{if}\ \mathsf{c}_{i,j}<0,\end{cases}

where

\mathsf{y}_{i,m}^{(k)}\mathbin{:=}\dfrac{\mathsf{y}_{i,m}^{k}}{[k]_{i}!}.

Now we are in a position to state our final main result of this paper.

Theorem 8.1
.

The automorphisms
\{\textbf{{T}}^{\pm 1}_{i}\ |\ i\in I\}
give a braid group
B(\triangle)
action on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
and hence on
\mathfrak{K}_{q}
.

Proof.

We give the proof of Theorem
8.1
in Section
8.2
.
∎

The rest of this subsection will be devoted to present consequences of Theorem
8.1
and its proof.

For
m\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, we set

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m}\mathbin{:=}\langle\mathsf{y}_{i,m}\ |\ i\in\triangle_{0}\rangle\subset\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n}).

Note that
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{-}(\mathfrak{g})
via the
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
-algebra isomorphism
\varsigma_{m}
such that
\varsigma_{m}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=f_{i}
.
From the proofs of the propositions in Section
8.2
, we obtain the following corollary:

Corollary 8.2
.

For
i,j\in I
and
0\leqslant p<h_{i,j}
, we have

\underbrace{\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}}_{\text{$p$-times}}(\mathsf{y}_{i}),\ \underbrace{\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}}_{\text{$p$-times}}(\mathsf{y}_{j})\in\langle\mathsf{y}_{i,m},\mathsf{y}_{j,m}\rangle\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m}.

For
w\in\mathsf{W}
with
(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\in I(w)
, set

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}^{\pm 1}_{w}\mathbin{:=}\textbf{{T}}^{\pm 1}_{i_{1}}\dots\textbf{{T}}^{\pm 1}_{i_{r}}.

Note that
\textbf{{T}}^{\pm 1}_{w}
is well-defined by Theorem
8.1
.

Proposition 8.3
.

For
w\in\mathsf{W}
and
\ell(ws_{i})=\ell(w)+1
. Then we have the following:

(1)

\textbf{{T}}_{w}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m}.

(2)

There exists
\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{Q}[[q^{1/2}]]\setminus\{0\}
such that

\varsigma_{m}(\textbf{{T}}^{\pm}_{w}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m}))=\mathbf{c}T^{\pm}_{w}(f_{i}).

Proof.

By the standard argument, there exists
j\in I
such that
\ell(ws_{j})=\ell(w)-1
and a unique
w^{\prime}\in\mathsf{W}
such that

\ell(w^{\prime}s_{j})=\ell(w^{\prime})+1,\quad\ell(w^{\prime}s_{i})=\ell(w^{\prime})+1\quad\text{ and }\quad w=w^{\prime}y

whether either

(a)

y=\underbrace{s_{i}s_{j}\cdots s_{j}}_{\text{$(h_{i,j}>p\geqslant 2)$-factors}}
and
\ell(w)=\ell(w^{\prime})+p
, or

(b)

y=\underbrace{s_{j}s_{i}\cdots s_{j}}_{\text{$(h_{i,j}>p\geqslant 1)$-factors}}
and
\ell(w)=\ell(w^{\prime})+p
.

With this observation, the first assertion now follows from an induction argument on
\ell(w)
and Corollary
8.2
(see
[
Lus93
, 40.1.2]
).
Next, Remark
7.5
implies that the second assertion holds for
w
with
\ell(w)=1
. In Propositions
8.6
,
8.8

and,
8.9
, we will see that

\varsigma_{m}(\textbf{{T}}^{\pm}_{y}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m}))=\mathbf{c}T^{\pm}_{y}(f_{i}).

Then the second assertion also follows from an induction argument on the length and (
1
).
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

(a)

For a reduced expression
{\underline{w}}=s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{r-1}}s_{i_{r}}
of
w
, if
s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{r-1}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i_{r}})={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}
, then

\textbf{{T}}^{\pm}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}^{\pm}_{i_{r-1}}(\mathsf{y}_{i_{r},m})=\mathsf{y}_{j,m}.

(b)

For
w_{\circ}\in\mathsf{W}
, we have

\textbf{{T}}^{\pm}_{w_{\circ}}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\mathsf{y}_{i^{*},m\pm 1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m\pm 1}.

Proof.

In this proof, we only consider
\textbf{{T}}_{i}
-case, since the proof for
\textbf{{T}}^{-}_{i}
-case is similar.

(
a
) Assigning
(-1)^{m}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}
for the weight of
\mathsf{y}_{i,m}
, the algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
is a
\Phi
-graded algebra. With this
\Phi
-grading, the automorphism
\textbf{{T}}_{i}
sends a
{\mspace{1mu}\beta}
-homogeneous element to a
s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})
-homogeneous element. Since
(-1)^{m}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}
-graded space in
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m}
is
1
-dimensional and spanned by
\mathsf{y}_{j,m}
, our assertion follows.

(
b
) Note that there exists
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell})\in I(w_{\circ})
such that
i_{\ell}=i
. Then we have
s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{\ell-1}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i})={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i^{*}}
and hence

\textbf{{T}}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{i_{\ell-1}}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\mathsf{y}_{i^{*},m}

by (
a
). Thus

\textbf{{T}}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{i_{\ell-1}}\textbf{{T}}_{i_{\ell}}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{i_{\ell-1}}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m+1})=\mathsf{y}_{i^{*},m+1},

as desired.
∎

Note that, for
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}=(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{\ell})\in I(w_{\circ})
, the set

\mathbb{P}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ},m}\mathbin{:=}\{\textbf{{T}}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{i_{k-1}}(\mathsf{y}_{i_{k},m})\ |\ 1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell\}\text{ is linearly independent and
$|\mathbb{P}_{\textbf{{i}}_{\circ},m}|=\ell$.}

For
\textbf{{i}}_{\circ}=(j_{1},j_{2},\ldots,j_{\ell})\in I(w_{\circ})
, we define
\widetilde{\textbf{{i}}}_{0}=(i_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{>0}}
as follows:

i_{k}=j_{k}\text{ for }1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell\quad\text{ and }\quad j_{k+\ell}=j_{k}^{*}.

Then Corollary
8.4
implies that

\{\textbf{{T}}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}_{i_{k-1}}(\mathsf{y}_{i_{k},m})\ |\ k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{>0}\}\sqcup\{\textbf{{T}}^{-}_{i_{1}}\cdots\textbf{{T}}^{-}_{i_{k-1}}(\mathsf{y}_{i_{k},m-1})\ |\ k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{>0}\}\text{ is linearly independent}

for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.

8.2.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

We investigate the braid relations (
8.1
) on
\left\{\textbf{{T}}_{i}\,|\,i\in I\,\right\}
in the next five propositions.

Proposition 8.5
.

Assume
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=0
. Then we have

\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}.

Proof.

We verify
\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})
for any pair
(k,m)\in I\times\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.
In the case of
\mathsf{c}_{i,k}\geqslant 0
and
\mathsf{c}_{j,k}\geqslant 0
,
it is straightforward to check it.
Let us consider the other case, that is,
\mathsf{c}_{i,k}<0
or
\mathsf{c}_{j,k}<0
.
Since
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=0
, it allows us to take a Dynkin quiver
Q
with sources
i
and
j
.
By Theorem
7.2
and Proposition
7.4
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})=L^{Q}_{q}(s_{i}s_{j}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{k}),m)=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m}).

Since
\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}
is invertible, we conclude
\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.6
.

For a simply-laced
\mathfrak{g}
, assume
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=-1
. Then we have

\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}.

Proof.

We claim that
\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})
for any pair
(k,m)\in I\times\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
.
If
i\not\sim k
and
j\not\sim k
, that is,
k
is not adjacent to both
i
and
j
on the Dynkin diagram, then we have
\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})
by definition.
Let us consider the other two cases:

Case 1
. Assume
k=i
or
k=j
.
We prove the case of
k=i
here, since the proof for the other case is quite similar.
Let
Q
be a Dynkin quiver with source
i
.
By similar computation to the proof of Proposition
7.4
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{i}\left(\dfrac{q^{1/2}\mathsf{y}_{i,m}\mathsf{y}_{j,m}-q^{-1/2}\mathsf{y}_{j,m}\mathsf{y}_{i,m}}{q-q^{-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{q^{1/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},m+1)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)-q^{-1/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},m+1)}{q-q^{-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{q^{1/2}L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},m)*L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)-q^{-1/2}L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)*L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},m)}{q-q^{-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m),

where
\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},m+1)=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},m)
and
\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{j,m})=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)=L_{q}^{s_{i}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},m)
.
This implies

(8.4)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\mathsf{y}_{j,m},

which prove the current case because (
8.4
) yields

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m+1})=\mathsf{y}_{j,m+1},\qquad\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{j,m})=\mathsf{y}_{j,m+1}.

Case 2
.
Assume
i\sim k
,
j\not\sim k
or
i\not\sim k
,
j\sim k
.
We only consider the former case, since the other case is proved in a similar way.
Under the assumption of
i\sim k
and
j\not\sim k
,
we have

(8.5)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{q^{1/2}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})\mathsf{y}_{j,m}-q^{-1/2}\mathsf{y}_{j,m}\textbf{{T}}_{i}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})}{q-q^{-1}}\right),

(8.6)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{q^{1/2}\mathsf{y}_{k,m}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})-q^{1/2}\textbf{{T}}_{j}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})\mathsf{y}_{k,m}}{q-q^{-1}}\right),

where (
8.5
) follows from (
8.4
).
To see the equality between (
8.5
) and (
8.6
),
it is enough to consider the case of
\mathfrak{g}=A_{3}
,
k=1
,
i=2
,
j=3
, and
m=0
.
Set
y_{t}\mathbin{:=}\mathsf{y}_{t,0}
for
t=1,2,3
.
Under this setting,
(
8.5
) and (
8.6
) become

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{1/2}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1})y_{3}-q^{-1/2}y_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1})}{q-q^{-1}}=\left(\dfrac{qy_{1}y_{2}y_{3}-y_{2}y_{1}y_{3}-y_{3}y_{1}y_{2}+q^{-1}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}}{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{1/2}y_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{3}(y_{2})-q^{1/2}\textbf{{T}}_{3}(y_{2})y_{1}}{q-q^{-1}}=\left(\dfrac{qy_{1}y_{2}y_{3}-y_{1}y_{3}y_{2}-y_{2}y_{3}y_{1}+q^{-1}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}}{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}\right),

respectively.
Since
y_{1}y_{3}=y_{3}y_{1}
by the defining relation of
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{m}
,
we prove our claim.
∎

Proposition 8.7
.

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}
,
C_{n}
or
F_{4}
, assume
\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=1
. Then we have

\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}=\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}.

Proof.

Since the proofs for
B_{n}
,
C_{n}
and
F_{4}
are all similar, we only give the proof when
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}
.
The case of
i,j\in I\setminus\{n-2,n-1\}
with
\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=1
follows from Proposition
8.6
, so
it is enough to prove that

\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m}).

We see that

(8.7)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n,m}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})^{2}-(q+q^{-1})\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})\mathsf{y}_{n,m}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})+q^{-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})^{2}\mathsf{y}_{n,m}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})},

while

(8.8)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n-2}\left(\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n,m}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}^{(2)}-\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}\mathsf{y}_{n,m}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}+q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}^{(2)}\mathsf{y}_{n,m}}{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\dagger}{=}\dfrac{q\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}^{2}-(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}+q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}^{2}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})},

where
\overset{\dagger}{=}
follows from (
8.4
).
To see the equality between (
8.7
) and (
8.8
), it suffices to assume that
n=3
and
m=0
. Set
y_{i}\mathbin{:=}\mathsf{y}_{i,0}
for
i=1,2,3
and recall that
q_{1}=q_{2}=q
, and
q_{3}=q^{2}
.
By multiplying the numerators of (
8.7
) and (
8.8
) by
(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})
, the first one becomes

(8.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big(qy_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(y_{2})^{2}-(q+q^{-1})\textbf{{T}}_{1}(y_{2})y_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(y_{2})+q^{-1}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(y_{2})^{2}y_{3}\big)\times(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})\\
&=\left\{q^{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}-qy_{3}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}-qy_{3}y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}+y_{3}y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}\right.\\
&\hskip 21.52771pt-(q+q^{-1})(qy_{2}y_{3}y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}-y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}-y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{3}y_{2}+q^{-1}y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}y_{3}y_{2})\\
&\hskip 64.58313pt\left.+y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}y_{3}-q^{-1}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}y_{3}-q^{-1}y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}y_{3}+q^{-2}y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}\right\}\times(q+q^{-1})\\
\end{split}

and the second one becomes

(8.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big(q\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{3})y_{1}^{2}-(q+q^{-1})y_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{3})y_{1}+q^{-1}y_{1}^{2}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{3})\big)\times(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})\\
&=\left\{q^{2}y_{3}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}^{2}-(q^{2}+1)y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2}y_{3}y_{1}^{2}\right.\\
&\hskip 21.52771pt-(q^{2}+1)y_{1}y_{3}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}+(q^{2}+2+q^{-2})y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}-(1+q^{-2})y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}y_{1}\\
&\hskip 64.58313pt\left.+y_{1}^{2}y_{3}y_{2}^{2}-(1+q^{-2})y_{1}^{2}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}+q^{-2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}\right\}.\\
\end{split}

Now, we claim that (
8.9
) and (
8.10
) coincide with each other.
By the quantum Serre relations (
7.7
), we have

(8.11)

(a)

y_{1}^{2}y_{2}-(q+q^{-1})y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}+y_{2}y_{1}^{2}=0\iff(q+q^{-1})y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}=y_{1}^{2}y_{2}+y_{2}y_{1}^{2}
,

(b)

y_{2}^{2}y_{1}-(q+q^{-1})y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}+y_{1}y_{2}^{2}=0\iff(q+q^{-1})y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}=y_{2}^{2}y_{1}+y_{1}y_{2}^{2}
.

Using (
8.11
) (
a
), (
b
) and the relation
y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}=y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}
,
the equality of (
8.9
) and (
8.10
) follows from the computation as shown below:

\displaystyle\left\{q^{2}y_{3}y_{2}\underline{y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}}-qy_{3}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}-qy_{3}y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}+y_{3}y_{1}\underline{y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt-(q+q^{-1})(qy_{2}y_{3}\underline{y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}}-y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}-y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{3}y_{2}+q^{-1}\underline{y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}}y_{3}y_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt\left.+y_{2}\underline{y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}}y_{3}-q^{-1}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}y_{3}-q^{-1}y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}y_{3}+q^{-2}\underline{y_{1}y_{2}y_{1}}y_{2}y_{3}\right\}\times\underline{(q+q^{-1})}

\displaystyle=q^{2}y_{3}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}^{2}-(q^{2}+1)y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2}y_{3}y_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt-(q^{2}+1)y_{1}y_{3}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}+(q^{2}+2+q^{-2})y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}-(1+q^{-2})y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}y_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt+y_{1}^{2}y_{3}y_{2}^{2}-(1+q^{-2})y_{1}^{2}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}+q^{-2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}+y_{3}(y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}-y_{2}y_{1}^{2}y_{2})

where all underlined monomials in
y_{i}

(1\leqslant i\leqslant 3)
with the factor
(q+q^{-1})
are replaced by (
8.11
), and then one can see that the last expression is equal to (
8.10
).
This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.8
.

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}
or
C_{n}

(
resp.
F_{4}
)
, we have

\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\qquad\text{$($resp. }\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{3}=\textbf{{T}}_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{2})

Proof.

Since the proofs for
B_{n}
,
C_{n}
and
F_{4}
are all similar, we only give the proof when
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}
.
We claim that

\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{k,m})

for all
1\leqslant k\leqslant n
. The case when
k<n-2
is obvious by definition.
Let us verify three non-trivial cases as follows, which prove our claim.

Case 1
.
Let
k=n-1
. For the time being, we omit
m+1
for simplicity of notation to write
\mathsf{y}_{i}=\mathsf{y}_{i,m+1}
and
L_{q}^{\widetilde{Q}}({\mspace{1mu}\beta})=L_{q}^{\widetilde{Q}}({\mspace{1mu}\beta},m+1)
for a Dynkin quiver
\widetilde{Q}
.
By taking a Dynkin quiver
Q
with source at
n-1
,

\displaystyle(q-q^{-1})\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})=(q-q^{-1})\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1})

\displaystyle=(q-q^{-1})\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\left(\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n-1}\mathsf{y}_{n}^{(2)}-\mathsf{y}_{n}\mathsf{y}_{n-1}\mathsf{y}_{n}+q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n}^{(2)}\mathsf{y}_{n-1}}{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+2)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})-q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+2)}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})

\displaystyle\,\,-L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*\left(\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+2)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})-q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+2)}{(q^{2}-q^{-2})}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{qL_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})-q^{-1}L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right)*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})

\displaystyle\,\,-L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*\left(\dfrac{qL_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})-q^{-1}L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1})}{(q^{2}-q^{-2})}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\ddagger}{=}L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})-L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}(q-q^{-1})L_{q}^{s_{n-1}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})=(q-q^{-1})L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})

(8.12)

\displaystyle\overset{\dagger}{=}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})-L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n}),

where
\overset{\ddagger}{=}
,
\overset{\star}{=}
, and

\overset{\dagger}{=}
follow from (
4.13
).
Since (
4.14
) tells us that

(8.13)

\displaystyle L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+1)-q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+1)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)}{q^{2}-q^{-2}},

we have

(8.14)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}^{(2)}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}-\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}+q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}^{(2)}}{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\textbf{{T}}_{n}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}),

by replacing
L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n})
in (
8.12
) with (
8.13
).
By (
8.14
), we have

(8.15)

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1})=\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1})=\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}.

and

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})

\displaystyle=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})=\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}.

Case 2
.
Let us consider
k=n
. Take a Dynkin quiver
Q
with source
n
.
By a similar argument as in
Case 1
with (
4.13
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n}\left(\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}-q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m+1}\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+2)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)-q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+2)}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{qL_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)*L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+1)-q^{-1}L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+1)*L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}

\displaystyle=L_{q}^{s_{n}Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{qL_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+1)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)-q^{-1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n},m+1)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{n-1},m+1)}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}).

Hence, it yields

\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})=\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1},

and

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})

\displaystyle=\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m-1})=\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n,m})=\mathsf{y}_{n,m+1}.

Case 3
.
Let us consider
k=n-2
.
We have

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\left(\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}-q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right)

(8.16)

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}\left(\dfrac{q\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}-q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right).

Here
\overset{\star}{=}
follows from (
8.15
).
On the other hand,

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})

\displaystyle=\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}\left(\dfrac{q\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}-q^{-1}\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m}\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m}}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right)

(8.17)

\displaystyle\overset{\dagger}{=}\left(\dfrac{q\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})\textbf{{T}}_{n}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})-q^{-1}\textbf{{T}}_{n}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-1,m})\textbf{{T}}_{n-1}(\mathsf{y}_{n-2,m})}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}\right).

Here
\overset{\dagger}{=}
follows from (
8.14
).
To see the equality between (
8.16
) and (
8.17
), it suffices to assume that
n=3
and
m=0
. Set
y_{i}\mathbin{:=}\mathsf{y}_{i,0}
for
i=1,2,3
and recall that
q_{1}=q_{2}=q^{2}
, and
q_{3}=q
.
By direct computation, we have

(8.18)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(q\textbf{{T}}_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1})y_{2}-q^{-1}y_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{3}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1}))-(q\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1})\textbf{{T}}_{3}^{-1}(y_{2})-q^{-1}\textbf{{T}}_{3}^{-1}(y_{2})\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1}))}{(q^{2}-q^{-2})}

\displaystyle=\left\{(q^{3}+q)(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{3}-y_{1}y_{3}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2})+(q-q^{-1})(y_{3}^{2}y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}-y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}^{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.22217pt+(q^{-1}+q^{-3})(y_{3}y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}-y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}y_{3}y_{1})+qy_{3}^{2}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}+q^{-1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}y_{3}^{2}-qy_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{2}-q^{-1}y_{3}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-2}.

Now we claim that (
8.18
) vanishes.
Note that

(8.19)

(a)

y_{2}^{2}y_{1}-(q^{2}+q^{-2})y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}+y_{1}y_{2}^{2}=0\iff(q^{2}+q^{-2})y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}=y_{2}^{2}y_{1}+y_{1}y_{2}^{2}
,

(b)

y_{2}^{2}y_{3}-(q^{2}+q^{-2})y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}+y_{3}y_{2}^{2}=0\iff(q^{2}+q^{-2})y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}=y_{2}^{2}y_{3}+y_{3}y_{2}^{2}
.

Then we have

\displaystyle\underline{(q^{2}+q^{-2})}\times\left\{(q^{3}+q)(y_{1}\underline{y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}}y_{3}-y_{1}y_{3}\underline{y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}})+(q-q^{-1})(y_{3}^{2}\underline{y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}}-\underline{y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}}y_{3}^{2})\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 17.22217pt\left.+(q^{-1}+q^{-3})(y_{3}\underline{y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}}y_{1}-\underline{y_{2}y_{3}y_{2}}y_{3}y_{1})+qy_{3}^{2}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}+q^{-1}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}y_{3}^{2}-qy_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{2}-q^{-1}y_{3}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle=(q^{3}+q)\left(y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{2}-y_{1}y_{3}^{2}y_{2}^{2}\right)\quad(\because~\eqref{eq: B3 q-serre}~\eqref{it: 23})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt+(q-q^{-1})\left(y_{3}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}+y_{3}^{2}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}-y_{2}^{2}y_{1}y_{3}^{2}-y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{2}\right)\quad(\because~\eqref{eq: B3 q-serre}~\eqref{it: 21})

\displaystyle\hskip 25.83325pt+(q^{-1}+q^{-3})\left(y_{3}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}-y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{2}y_{1}\right)\quad(\because~\eqref{eq: B3 q-serre}~\eqref{it: 23})

\displaystyle\hskip 38.74988pt+(q^{3}+q^{-1})y_{3}^{2}y_{1}y_{2}^{2}+(q+q^{-3})y_{2}^{2}y_{1}y_{3}^{2}-(q^{3}+q^{-1})y_{1}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{2}-(q+q^{-3})y_{3}^{2}y_{2}^{2}y_{1}

\displaystyle=0,

as desired,
where all underlined monomials in
y_{i}

(1\leqslant i\leqslant 3)
with the factor
(q^{2}+q^{-2})
are replaced by (
8.19
).
∎

Proposition 8.9
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=G_{2}
, we have

\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}=\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}.

Proof.

It suffices to show that

\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{i,m})\qquad(i=1,2).

In this proof, we omit
*
-operation and
m+1
frequently for simplicity of notation. Note that
q_{1}=q
and
q_{2}=q^{3}
.
Let us take a Dynkin quiver
Q^{\prime}
with source
2
and
\xi_{2}=5
whose
\Gamma^{Q^{\prime}}
is described as

and set
Q\mathbin{:=}s_{2}Q^{\prime}
.
We remark that the height function
\xi
of
Q
is given by
\xi_{1}=4
and
\xi_{2}=3

(cf. (
A
) below for
\Gamma^{Q}
).
Then we have

(8.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(\mathsf{y}_{2,m})=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2})\\
&=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{2}\left(\dfrac{q^{3/2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}+q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}-q^{-3/2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}\mathsf{y}_{2}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})}\right)\\
&=\left\{q^{3/2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m+2)L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{3}-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m+2)L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{2}\right.\\
&\hskip 25.83325pt\left.-q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m+2)L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})+q^{-3/2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{3}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m+2)\right\}\\
&\hskip 232.49927pt\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-1}\\
&=\left\{q^{3/2}L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{3}-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{2}\right.\\
&\hskip 137.77734pt\left.-q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{2}L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})+q^{-3/2}L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{3}L^{Q}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})\right\}\\
&\hskip 232.49927pt\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-1}\\
&=L^{Q}_{q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})=L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})\\
&=\dfrac{q^{-1/2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})-q^{1/2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})}{q-q^{-1}},\end{split}

by (
4.13
).
Since

\displaystyle L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})=\dfrac{q^{3/2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})-q^{-3/2}L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})}{q^{3}-q^{-3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 77.49976pt\iff\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}^{-1}(L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}))=\dfrac{q^{3/2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{-3/2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}}{q^{3}-q^{-3}},

\displaystyle L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})=\dfrac{q^{1/2}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})-q^{-1/2}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 77.49976pt\iff\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}^{-1}(L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}))=\left(\dfrac{q^{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}-(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}}{(q^{3}-q^{-3})(q^{2}-q^{-2})}\right),

the equality (
8.20
) implies that

(8.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(\mathsf{y}_{2,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2})\\
&=\left(q^{-1/2}(q^{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}-(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2})(q^{3/2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{-3/2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1})\right.\\
&\hskip 21.52771pt\left.-q^{1/2}(q^{3/2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{-3/2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1})(q^{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}-(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2})\right)\\
&\hskip 193.74939pt\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-2}\\
&=\left\{q^{3}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{2}(q^{2}+1+q^{-2})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}+(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}\mathsf{y}_{2}\right.\\
&\hskip 21.52771pt+q^{2}(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}-(1+q^{-2}+q^{-4})\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-3}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\\
&\hskip 43.05542pt\left.-\mathsf{y}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}-(q^{3}+q^{-3})\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}^{2})\mathsf{y}_{1}\right\}\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-2}\\
&\overset{\dagger}{=}\left\{q^{3}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{2}(q^{2}+1+q^{-2})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}+(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}\mathsf{y}_{2}\right.\\
&\hskip 21.52771pt\left.+q^{2}(q+q^{-1})\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}-(1+q^{-2}+q^{-4})\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-3}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\right\}\\
&\hskip 193.74939pt\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-2}\\
&=\dfrac{q^{3}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}+[2]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{-2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{2}[2]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-3}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{2}},\end{split}

where
\overset{\dagger}{=}
follows from (
7.7
).
Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2})=L^{Q^{\prime}}_{q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q^{3}\mathcal{K}^{2}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}-q^{2}[3]_{1}\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}^{2}\mathcal{R}+[2]_{1}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}^{3}\mathcal{R}-q^{-2}[3]_{1}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}^{2}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}+q^{2}[2]_{1}\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}+q^{-3}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{K}^{2}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{2}}

where

\mathcal{K}\mathbin{:=}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})
and
\mathcal{R}\mathbin{:=}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})
.
Here one can observe that

(8.22)

\begin{split}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m+2)&=L_{q}^{s_{2}Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m+2)=L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m+2)=\mathscr{S}_{-2}(L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})),\\
L_{q}^{Q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})&=L_{q}^{s_{2}Q^{\prime}}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})=L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})=\mathscr{S}_{-2}(L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})).\end{split}

where
\mathscr{S}_{-2}
is defined in (
2.12
).
By (
8.21
) and (
8.22
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(\mathsf{y}_{2,m})=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2}))

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q^{3}K^{2}RKR-q^{2}[3]_{1}KRK^{2}R+[2]_{1}RK^{3}R-q^{-2}[3]_{1}RK^{2}RK+q^{2}[2]_{1}KRKRK+q^{-3}RKRK^{2}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{2}},

where

K\mathbin{:=}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m+2)=\mathscr{S}_{-2}(L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}))
and
R\mathbin{:=}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}+3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})=\mathscr{S}_{-2}(L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}))
.
Thus we have

(8.23)

\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2})=\mathscr{S}_{-2}(L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}))=L_{q}^{Q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}).

On the other hand, we have

(8.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{2}^{-1}\textbf{{T}}_{1}^{-1}(\mathsf{y}_{2})\\
&=\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}^{-1}_{2}\left(\dfrac{q^{3/2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}\mathsf{y}_{2}-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}+q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1}\mathsf{y}_{2,m}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{2}-q^{-3/2}\mathsf{y}_{2}\mathsf{y}_{1}^{3}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})}\right)\\
&=\left\{q^{3/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{3}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})\right.\\
&\hskip 25.83325pt\left.+q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{2}-q^{-3/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})^{3}\right\}\\
&\hskip 223.88818pt\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-1}\\
&=\left\{q^{3/2}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{3}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{2}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})\right.\\
&\hskip 68.88867pt\left.+q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{2}-q^{-3/2}L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})^{3}\right\}\\
&\hskip 223.88818pt\times(q-q^{-1})^{-1}(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{-1}(q^{3}-q^{-3})^{-1}\\
&=L_{q}^{Q^{\prime}}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2})=L_{q}^{Q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}).\end{split}

By combining (
8.23
) with (
8.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{2,m})=y_{2,m+1}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(y_{2,m})=\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{2,m-1})=\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{2,m})=y_{2,m+1}.

Similarly, one can see that

\displaystyle\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}(y_{1,m})=y_{1,m+1}=\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}\textbf{{T}}_{2}\textbf{{T}}_{1}(y_{1,m}),

as desired. We complete the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

In Propositions
8.5
–
8.9
, we have shown that

\displaystyle\underbrace{\textbf{{T}}_{i}\textbf{{T}}_{j}\cdots}_{\text{ $h_{i,j}$-times}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\textbf{{T}}_{j}\textbf{{T}}_{i}\cdots}_{\text{ $h_{i,j}$-times}},

which amounts to a proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix A
Interpretation of
\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2,m})
when
\mathfrak{g}=G_{2}

Recall that
q_{1}=q
,
q_{2}=q^{3}
and

\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2,m})=\dfrac{q^{3/2}\mathsf{y}_{2,m}\mathsf{y}_{1,m}^{3}-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1,m}\mathsf{y}_{2,m}\mathsf{y}_{1,m}^{2}+q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{y}_{1,m}^{2}\mathsf{y}_{2,m}\mathsf{y}_{1,m}-q^{-3/2}\mathsf{y}_{1,m}^{3}\mathsf{y}_{2,m}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})}.

with source

and

, whose

From the viewpoint of Corollary
5.3
,
the relation (
4.14
) tells us that

(i)

\dfrac{q^{-1/2}L_{q}^{Q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m)-q^{1/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m)*L_{q}^{Q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)}{q-q^{-1}}=L_{q}^{Q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)
,

(ii)

\dfrac{q^{1/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m)-q^{-1/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)}{q^{2}-q^{-2}}=L_{q}^{Q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)
,

(iii)

\dfrac{q^{3/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m)-q^{-3/2}L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1},m)*L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)}{q^{3}-q^{-3}}=L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)
,

since

(i)
′

p_{2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}=2
and
(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})=1
,

(ii)
′

p_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}=1
and
({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})=-1
,

(iii)
′

p_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}=0
and
({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1})=-3
,

respectively. Replacing
L_{q}^{Q}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)
in (
ii
) with the LHS of (
iii
) and then replacing
L_{q}^{Q}(2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)
in (
i
) with the already-replaced one, we have

\displaystyle L_{q}^{Q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q^{3/2}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{2,m}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{1,m})^{3}-q^{1/2}[3]_{1}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{1,m}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{2,m}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{1,m})^{2}+q^{-1/2}[3]_{1}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{1,m})^{2}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{2,m}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{1,m}-q^{-3/2}(\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{1,m})^{3}\mathsf{x}^{Q}_{2,m}}{(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})(q^{3}-q^{-3})}

and hence

\widetilde{\Uptheta}_{Q}\circ\textbf{{T}}_{1}(\mathsf{y}_{2,m})=L_{q}^{Q}(3{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2},m).
